# Repository–literature bridge

```text
Status: IMPORTED literature pointers with independent verification; no change to integrated repository results is proposed here
Scope: the integrated programmes summarized in research/integrated/CURRENT_RESULTS.md (host repository)
Exact sources or dependencies: per item
What was actually run: one analyst per programme (reading repository files + literature) and one independent verifier per programme
Smallest remaining gap: items marked unverifiable / relation_overstated; repository maintainers should review before citing
```

This bridge maps each repository programme to (a) prior art for its retained statements, (b) known obstructions, (c) transplantable tools. Per AGENTS.md, any consequence for repository statements is a **proposal for the repository's own review**; repository acceptance never implied external novelty, and this file does not change any repository status.


## CAUSAL_EULER

**Programme scope examined:** CAUSAL_EULER: horizon-preserving causal Euler/Dickman corrections (CURRENT_RESULTS.md section 9, OPEN_CUTS.md section 6, PROGRAMMES.md section 6, frozen PR #793 pass7 proof at cdf2f15965decbd35afbbd09a16c89cb3e215737)

### Known obstructions

- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-O1** The OPEN_CUTS section 6 target is equivalent to RH, and its sigma0-version is equivalent to the open quasi-RH problem. By Akatsuka Thm 2, for each sigma0 in (1/2,1), convergence of the corrected product to a nonzero limit at even one point of the line Re s=sigma0 (m=0) is equivalent to zeta!=0 on Re s>sigma0, and to psi(x)=x+O(x^{sigma0}). Together with the repository's DC6 Montel argument, local uniform boundedness on compacta of Re s>sigma0 is equivalent to zeta!=0 there. — *Akatsuka, Kodai Math. J. 40 (2017), Theorems 2-3; repository PROOF.md DC6 (cdf2f159...)*. How it constrains: No contribution to section 6 can be unconditional unless it proves a zero-free half-plane; even Theta<1 is open. Any claimed 'signed discrepancy estimate with explicit dependence on distance from the critical line' at fixed s with sigma<1 is either conditional on a zero-location hypothesis or proves quasi-RH. The section 6 target should not be listed as a bounded contribution. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-O2** There are Omega-plus/minus lower bounds for the corrected-product discrepancy. For fixed real s>-2, log G1(s,x) = Omega-plus/minus(x^{Theta-s-eps}) (Landau's oscillation theorem). Combined with Littlewood's psi(x)-x = Omega-plus/minus(sqrt x logloglog x) and (2.15), log G1(s,x) = Omega-plus/minus(x^{1/2-s} logloglog x/log x); the arXiv text of Cor. 2.10 prints 'log log x'. — *Gorodetsky arXiv:2211.08973, Lemma 2.8, Corollary 2.10, eq. (2.15); Littlewood via Montgomery-Vaughan Thm 15.11*. How it constrains: Even under RH, no bound for log(D/P-check_X) better than about X^{1/2-sigma}logloglog X/log X is possible. The repository's RH-conditional rate O(X^{1/2-sigma}log X) is optimal up to roughly log^2 X. If Theta>1/2, the discrepancy is unbounded at real s<Theta. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-O3** The Dickman/pole correction is a low-height device. Since e^{-E1(z)} = 1 - e^{-z}/z(1+O(1/\|z\|)), C_X(sigma+it) = 1+O(X^{1-sigma}/(\|t\| log X)) once \|t\| log X >> X^{1-sigma}. By GHK eq. (13) (sharp limit) and Gorodetsky Cor. 5.4, the whole remaining discrepancy log(D/P-check_X) is the zero sum sum_rho E1((s-rho)log X) plus trivial-zero and prime-power terms. — *GHK arXiv:math/0511182 eq. (13); Gorodetsky arXiv:2211.08973 Corollary 5.4; DLMF 6.12 (E1 asymptotics); derivation is elementary*. How it constrains: The causal correction exactly removes the pole contribution and carries no information about zeros. Any half-plane progress must control sum_rho E1((s-rho)L), the classical explicit-formula zero sum. Positivity of nu=-rho' cannot help there. [fulltext_read (GHK identity) plus elementary derivation; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-O4** Zero-counting obstruction to uniform approximation. Finite Euler products have no zeros in Re s>0. C_X = e^gamma z e^{-Ein(z)} vanishes only at s=1, so P-check_X is zero-free on Re s>0 except at s=1, while D has poles at the zeros of zeta. — *Gonek, Trans. AMS 364 (2012), arXiv:0704.3448, remark after Theorem 3.5*. How it constrains: Convergence P-check_X->D can hold at best locally on Re s>1/2. There is no uniform approximation on any region meeting the critical line, and the boundary line requires a DRH-type pointwise statement (O6). [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-O5** D=1/(zeta(s)(1-q^{-s})) is unbounded on every vertical line Re s=sigma in (1/2,1]. Bohr-Courant: zeta(sigma+it) is dense in C for 1/2<sigma<=1. Quantitative Omega results: Montgomery 1977 under RH; resonance/Diophantine lower bounds for the Selberg class (Aistleitner-Pankowski). — *H. Bohr, R. Courant, J. reine angew. Math. 144 (1914); H. L. Montgomery, Comment. Math. Helv. 52 (1977); C. Aistleitner, L. Pankowski, 'Large values of L-functions from the Selberg class', arXiv:1507.06066 (J. Math. Anal. Appl. 2017)*. How it constrains: Any explicit-dependence estimate must be local in height (compact K), with constants growing at least as fast as extreme values of 1/zeta. A bound uniform on vertical lines is false. [memory_unverified (Bohr-Courant, Montgomery); abstract_only (Aistleitner-Pankowski); verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-O6** On Re s=1/2 the corrected product cannot converge to D without extra structure. There is a sqrt 2 factor at t=0, coming from the prime-square tail, which tends to -(1/2)log 2. Convergence on the line is equivalent to psi(x)=x+o(sqrt x log x), the Deep Riemann Hypothesis, which is beyond RH. — *Akatsuka 2017, Theorem 1 and Lemma 2.1; Conrad 2005, Theorems 1.2, 5.11, 6.2; Kimura-Koyama-Kurokawa, Lett. Math. Phys. 104 (2014), arXiv:1210.1216, Conjecture 2; Kaneko arXiv:1902.04203, Case II*. How it constrains: The repository's prime-power convention bound O_sigma(X^{1/2-sigma}log X) degenerates at sigma=1/2. Any extension of the causal completion to the critical line must carry the sqrt 2 normalization and is a DRH-strength statement. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-O7** Montgomery refuted Turan's hypothesis: additive truncations sum_{n<=N} n^{-s} have zeros in sigma>1 (for large N, near sigma = 1+(4/pi-1-eps)loglog N/log N). — *H. L. Montgomery, 'Zeros of approximations to the zeta function', Studies in Pure Mathematics (Mem. P. Turan), 1983, 497-506*. How it constrains: Zero-freeness and positivity of truncations depend on whether the truncation is multiplicative. The repository's source M_X is a smooth-supported (Euler-product) truncation, so this does not hit DC1-DC5. It warns against re-expressing the corrected product through Dirichlet-polynomial truncations. [memory_unverified (bibliographic data confirmed via zbMATH); verifier: confirmed]

### Transplantable tools

- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-T1** Saias / Hildebrand-Tenenbaum Lemma 3.4 with the Vinogradov-Korobov zero-free region: zeta(s,y)=F(s,y)(1+O(1/L_eps(y))) uniformly in an X-dependent neighbourhood of sigma=1 with \|tau\|<=L_eps(y) (*Hildebrand-Tenenbaum, JTNB 5 (1993), Lemma 3.4; Saias, J. Number Theory 32 (1989); Gorodetsky arXiv:2211.08973 Lemma 2.9*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS section 6, read as 'any honest unconditional statement below Re s=1'. Next step: Restate in repository normalization: P-check_X(s)=D(s)/G(s,X) and \|log G(s,X)\| << 1/L_eps(X) for 1-(log X)^{-kappa}<=sigma<=2 and \|t\|<=L_eps(X). Pin kappa and L_eps from the primary source. Record it as the unconditional moving-region extension of DC5 with explicit distance (log X)^{-kappa} from Re s=1. Do not claim fixed-compact boundedness.. Risk: The region shrinks to Re s=1 as X grows, so it cannot feed the Montel/Vitali argument of DC6 (O1). The exact region (3.13) must be checked against the scanned primary source, since its formulas did not extract.
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-T2** Gorodetsky's signed-discrepancy machinery: log(D/P-check_X)=log G1+log G2; a zero-sum explicit formula (Cor. 5.4); a conditional size (2.15) in terms of psi(x)-x and Theta; a sharp prime-power size (Prop. 2.12); Omega-plus/minus sharpness (Lemma 2.8) (*O. Gorodetsky arXiv:2211.08973 (sections 2.2, 5); arXiv:2212.01949 Lemma 2.8, Corollary 2.9, Lemma 2.11 (complex s under condition min\|rho-s-t\|>>1)*). Target gap: The OPEN_CUTS section 6 'concrete output: a signed discrepancy estimate with explicit dependence on distance from the critical line'. Next step: Write a reviewable conditional theorem in the repository's exact conventions (q=67 factor, full local factors, single-valued C_X). Hypothesis: zeta!=0 on Re s>Theta. Conclusion: for every compact K in {Re s>Theta}, sup_K \|log(P-check_X/D)\| <<_K X^{Theta-sigma_K}(log X)^{A} + X^{1/2-sigma_K}/((sigma_K-1/2)log X). Also state the matching Omega-plus/minus lower bound. Import the complex-s version via 2212.01949 Lemma 2.8.. Risk: Purely conditional: the explicit dependence is in terms of the unknown Theta, so this repackages known results and makes no RH progress. Under the 'explicit dependence' wording it must not be presented as unconditional.
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-T3** GHK / Bombieri-Hejhal smoothing: replace the sharp prime cutoff by weights v(e^{log n/log X}) from a C-infinity u supported on [e^{1-1/X},e]. This gives the exact unconditional identity log zeta = smoothed prime sum - sum_rho U((s-rho)log X) + U((s-1)log X) - trivial terms, with absolutely convergent zero sums. (*GHK arXiv:math/0511182, Lemma 1 and eqs. (10)-(13)*). Target gap: Turning DC5/DC6 into an exact, absolutely convergent signed-discrepancy identity for the whole corrected product, valid for all Re s>=0 off zeros. Next step: Define C^(u)_X(s)=exp(-U((s-1)log X)). It is entire with a simple zero at s=1 because U(z)=-gamma-log z-int u(x) loglog x dx+int u(x)Ein(z log x)dx. Prove P-check^(u)_X = D exp(-sum_rho U((s-rho)L) - sum_m U((s+2m)L)) times explicit prime-power and q factors. Check that horizon faithfulness survives up to the shell n in (X^{1-1/X},X], then redo the DC5 tail estimate using the decay of U.. Risk: Smoothing perturbs coefficients near the horizon, so the exact (16) becomes exact only up to a thin shell, which needs a new review identity. The identity is exact, but the half-plane bound still needs zero information (O1, O3).
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-T4** Akatsuka's Abel-theorem plus Weierstrass route (proof of Theorem 3): pointwise convergence of the partial Dirichlet series on one vertical line gives uniform convergence on sectors, hence holomorphy on the half-plane, including the multiplicity-m normalization (*Akatsuka, Kodai Math. J. 40 (2017), Theorem 3 and sections 2-4*). Target gap: The DC6 criterion, which currently needs local boundedness on all compacta (Montel). Next step: Replace or supplement DC6's Montel argument by citing Akatsuka Thm 2. A single nonzero limit of P-check_X(s0) at one s0 with Re s0=sigma0 (and zeta(s0)!=0) already forces zeta!=0 on Re s>sigma0. Record Akatsuka's (1.9) as the explicit converse rate.. Risk: Citation and sharpening only. It weakens the hypothesis of the criterion, but the criterion remains RH-equivalent.
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-T5** Coefficient-side smooth-number templates: Saias's Psi(x,y)=Lambda(x,y)(1+O(exp(-(log y)^{a-eps}))); Hildebrand's RH-equivalence Psi(x,y) comparable to x rho(u) for y>=(log x)^{2+eps}; Weingartner's smooth/rough error-term link via Mobius inversion over smooth numbers (*Hildebrand, Mathematika 31 (1984) 258-271 (quoted in Gorodetsky arXiv:2212.01949 section 1); Saias 1989; Weingartner arXiv:2604.22058*). Target gap: A causal (coefficient/time-domain) formulation of the half-plane question for F-check_{X,a}, as an alternative to frequency-side local boundedness. Next step: Formulate the Mobius analogue: compare M_X(y) with its Dickman-corrected main term (the inverse transform of D C_X^{-1}... restricted to y in [X, X^{u}]). Identify which y-ranges correspond to RH via the Hildebrand mechanism, and test whether DC4's Buchstab profile is the leading term of a known M(x,y) asymptotic.. Risk: The analogous ranges are expected to be RH-equivalent or DRH-like (as Hildebrand's are), so this reformulates the problem rather than easing it. The M(x,y) literature (Alladi 1982; de la Breteche-Tenenbaum 2005) was not verified here.
- **BRG-CAUSAL_EULER-T6** Vertical-average (Bohr / Selberg) approximation: for fixed sigma>1/2 the truncated Euler product approximates log zeta in mean square over t in [T,2T] for X=T^{delta}, unconditionally via zero-density estimates (*Bohr-Jessen theory; Selberg's method (e.g. Titchmarsh ch. 11/14; Ingham's density N(sigma,T) << T^{3(1-sigma)/(2-sigma)}log^5 T)*). Target gap: An unconditional averaged substitute for fixed-compact boundedness in section 6. Next step: State and prove (1/T)int_T^{2T} \|log(P-check_X/D)(sigma+it)\|^2 dt -> 0 for X=T^{delta(sigma)}, noting C_X=1+O(X^{1-sigma}/(T log X)) at these heights.. Risk: By O3 the Dickman correction is negligible at these heights, so this is a statement about raw Euler products and does not use the causal structure. Averages give no control near individual zeros or at fixed compact K. Citations here are from memory.

### Novelty assessment

Classical or known, with the relation checked against full text where marked: (a) The correction factor itself. C_X(s) = e^gamma z e^{-Ein(z)} = z rho-hat(z) with z=(s-1)log X is exactly the de Bruijn-Saias factor in F(s,y)=zeta(s)(s-1)log y rho-hat((s-1)log y). The repository's corrected product satisfies P-check_X = D/G(s,X), where G=zeta(s,y)/F(s,y) is the ratio studied by Saias (1989), Hildebrand-Tenenbaum (1993) and Gorodetsky (2022-23). This is object-level identity, not analogy (PA1). (b) The Dickman Laplace transform (13) is HT 1993 Lemma 2.6, and the Buchstab duality 1+omega-hat = e^{E1} = 1/(z rho-hat) used in DC4 is also classical; I verified both numerically (PA2, PA9). (c) The pointwise line-one cancellation behind DC5 is a weaker, de la Vallee Poussin-strength form of Saias's lemma (HT Lemma 3.4) (PA3). The convergence of raw products on Re s=1 is classical (Mertens, Landau/Titchmarsh section 3.15). (d) The logarithmic derivative (17)/(18) is the sharp-cutoff pole term of the explicit formula. GHK (eq. 13) and Gonek (Thm 3.1, via Titchmarsh 14.20) carry the smoothed analogue and discard it at height \|t\|>=2 (PA6). (e) The DC6 criterion is a locally-uniform variant of Akatsuka 2017 Thm 2-3. His normalizer is exactly C_X/(e^gamma(s-1)), checked numerically. His theorem is sharper: one point on a vertical line already suffices. The pole-free versions are Conrad 2005 Thm 3.3 and Kaneko. The RH-conditional converse is a weaker form of Ramanujan's (HCN section 68) formula; the repository's entire C_X even reproduces Ramanujan's minus sign, since C_X(s) = -exp(li(X^{1-s})) for real s<1 (PA4, PA5, PA7). (f) DC6's remark on the divergent absolute majorant is quantified by Gorodetsky's (2.14) and is Omega-sharp by his Lemma 2.8 / Cor 2.10 (PA8, O2).  Plausibly new as stated, though I did not run an exhaustive search: the causal, logarithmic-time, full-frequency L2 packaging on Re s=1. That covers DC3's exact leading norm constant sqrt(C_g)/log X for the raw Mobius/Euler truncation error, DC4's L^{-3/2} Buchstab-derivative second profile, and DC5's exp(-c sqrt log X) full-frequency norm rate for the horizon-faithful corrected source. It also covers the operator-side observations: the norm is at most 2, it is not a contraction, and \|C_X(1+2i/L)\| = exp(Ci(2)), which I verified numerically. These follow routinely from classical inputs plus Plancherel. Their value is in exact constants, conventions and the coefficient-exact horizon property, which is itself the Mobius-side analogue of de Bruijn's Lambda(x,y) being exact for x<=y (PA10). A pointwise analogue of DC4 (a heuristic M(x,y) ~ x omega'(u)/log^2 y for the q-free sum) may already exist in Alladi 1982 or de la Breteche-Tenenbaum, which I could not verify.  Not new and not a bounded problem: the OPEN_CUTS section 6 target. By Akatsuka Thm 2 and DC6 it is RH-equivalent, and each sigma0-version is equivalent to the open zero-free half-plane problem (O1). The requested 'signed discrepancy estimate with explicit dependence on distance from the critical line' exists in the literature only conditionally, in terms of Theta (Gorodetsky, Ramanujan/Akatsuka), with matching Omega-plus/minus lower bounds.  I found no explicit witness that any integrated repository statement is wrong. Uncertainties: the exact region in HT Lemma 3.4 (scanned formulas did not extract); the Ramanujan formula, which I read only via Kaneko; and the Alladi / de la Breteche-Tenenbaum M(x,y) results, which I did not check.

### Recommended actions

- Cite the smooth-number lineage for proof eqs. (13)-(14) and CURRENT_RESULTS section 9: de Bruijn 1951, Saias 1989, Hildebrand-Tenenbaum 1993 (Lemma 2.6 and Lemma 3.4), and Gorodetsky arXiv:2211.08973 / 2212.01949. Record the identity P-check_X(s) = D(s)/G(s,X) with G = zeta(s,X)/F(s,X) and F(s,y) = rho-hat((s-1)log y) zeta(s)(s-1)log y. Say explicitly that the pointwise line-one cancellation is Saias's lemma at weaker strength; any novelty is the causal full-frequency L2 packaging and the DC3/DC4 constants.
- Cite Akatsuka 2017 (Kodai Math. J. 40, Theorems 2-3) as the 'classical completed Euler-product criterion' in DC6. Record that his normalizer exp[lim(int_{1+eps}^x du/(u^s log u) - log(1/eps))] equals C_X(s)/(e^gamma (s-1)), and that one nonzero pointwise limit on a vertical line Re s=sigma0 already forces zeta!=0 on Re s>sigma0.
- Re-label OPEN_CUTS section 6 as RH-equivalent, and the sigma0-version as equivalent to zeta!=0 on Re s>sigma0 (quasi-RH), so it is not read as a bounded contribution. Note that the conditional 'signed discrepancy with explicit dependence' is already in Gorodetsky (Cor. 5.4, (2.15), Prop. 2.12) and Ramanujan/Akatsuka, with Omega-plus/minus sharpness (Gorodetsky Lemma 2.8, Cor. 2.10).
- In PROOF.md section 7 (cutoff-convention tail), cite Akatsuka Lemma 2.1 / Gorodetsky Prop. 2.12 for the sharp size X^{1/2-sigma}/((sigma-1/2)log X) in place of O_sigma(X^{1/2-sigma}log X). Add a 'known misreading' note: at sigma=1/2 the tail tends to the constant (1/2)log 2, producing the sqrt 2 factor (Conrad 2005; Akatsuka Thm 1), so line-1/2 extensions are DRH-strength.
- Cite GHK 2007 eq. (13) and Gonek 2012 Thm 3.1 (Titchmarsh Thm 14.20) for eqs. (17)-(18). The repository's C_X is the exponentiated pole term exp(-U((s-1)log X)) of GHK in the sharp limit u->delta_e, kept exactly rather than bounded as O(X^{K+2}/(\|s\|log X)^K).
- Add to the programme's analytic boundaries the low-height nature of the correction: C_X(sigma+it) = 1+O(X^{1-sigma}/(\|t\|log X)). For \|t\| >> X^{1-sigma}/log X the corrected product equals the raw finite Euler product, and the full residual is the zero sum sum_rho E1((s-rho)log X). The positivity of nu=-rho' therefore cannot supply zero information.
- For a genuinely bounded contribution, choose one of two routes. (i) Prove the unconditional moving-region extension of DC5 (tool T1): Saias/HT Lemma 3.4 in repository normalization, with explicit (log X)^{-kappa} distance from Re s=1 and \|t\|<=L_eps(X). (ii) Write the conditional explicit-dependence theorem (tool T2) under the hypothesis zeta!=0 on Re s>Theta, together with its Omega-plus/minus lower bound. Label both clearly as non-RH-progress.
- Before claiming novelty for DC3/DC4, check Alladi (J. Number Theory 14 (1982) 86-98; Trans. AMS 272 (1982) 87-105) and de la Breteche-Tenenbaum (Invent. Math. 159 (2005); Ramanujan J. 9 (2005)) for asymptotics of M(x,y)=sum_{n<=x, P+(n)<=y} mu(n). DC4 implies heuristically M(x,y) ~ x omega'(u)/(log y)^2 in the q-free case.
- Question for the authors (not an error claim): DC6's converse asserts local uniform convergence on Re s>1/2 under RH via 'the discrepancy formula'. For complex s with sigma<1 the formula -int_X^inf t^{-s} d(psi(t)-t)/log t holds by analytic continuation from Re s>1; Gorodetsky's Lemma 2.11 states it only for real s>Theta. Could one line be added justifying the complex-s identity, for example via the identity theorem on Re s>1/2 under RH?
- Clarify in research/integrated/dickman_bellman/README.md, or in the RESULTS index, that the ARITH.DICKMAN.* rows (P61 Stieltjes transfer and Bellman corridor) are a separate family from the causal Euler/Dickman correction of CURRENT_RESULTS section 9, so that a grep for 'dickman' does not conflate the two.

### Independent verifier

The draft is careful and mostly accurate. Repository statements match the frozen PROOF.md at cdf2f159, B REPORT section 10-11, OPEN_CUTS section 6, PROGRAMMES section 6, RESULTS and the dickman_bellman CLAIMS.tsv. The central identification holds at object level (PA1): C_X=z rho-hat(z) and P-check_X=D/G(s,X). The Akatsuka normalizer identity (PA4) also holds, and both were independently re-verified, analytically and numerically to 30 digits. So were \|C_X(1+2i/L)\|=exp(Ci(2)) and C_X=-exp(li(X^{1-s})) for real s<1. I upgraded HT 1993 Lemmas 2.6 and 3.4 to primary evidence by rendering the scanned pages: kappa=2/5+eps and L_eps=exp((log y)^{3/5-eps}).  Substantive errors to fix before any of this is used:  1. Recommended action 4 and the PA5 note are wrong and would cause a silent false strengthening. PROOF.md section 7's cutoff tail sum_{p<=X,k>=2,p^k>X}(log p)p^{-ks} is log p-weighted. Numerically and analytically its sharp size is X^{1/2-sigma}/(2sigma-1), with no 1/log X. Akatsuka Lemma 2.1 and Gorodetsky Prop. 2.12 bound the different 1/k-weighted tail, which has size X^{1/2-sigma}/((2sigma-1)log X). The repository is loose by one log there, not log^2. DC6's overall RH rate O_K(X^{1/2-sigma}log X) is the standard order and matches Gorodetsky 2212.01949 Lemma 2.11 and Cor. 2.9.  2. PA6 and recommended action 5 overstate the contrast. Gonek 2012 explicitly keeps the pole factor in P*_X=P_X exp(-F2((s-1)log X)).  3. PA11's 'repo_generalizes' is wrong in kind. The L^2 statement does not contain the pointwise classical results; it uses them.  4. Conrad Thm 3.3's hypotheses are misdescribed: holomorphy is a conclusion there, not an assumption. The Euler-product critical-line equivalence is Thm 6.3.  5. O1 and recommended action 3 imply OPEN_CUTS section 6 is listed as a bounded contribution. It is not, and DC6 already records both RH directions. The useful change is to state the equivalence explicitly in the public text. Also, Akatsuka's single-point criterion needs a nonzero limit, not boundedness.  6. Recommended action 9's question already has a literature answer: Gorodetsky Cor. 5.4 and 2212.01949 Lemma 2.8 / Cor. 2.9 cover complex s.  Remaining uncertainties: - The Tenenbaum-book locations (PA9) and Montgomery's 1983 constant (O7) are unverified. - Whether a pointwise M(x,y) ~ c x omega'(u)/log^2 y result exists (Alladi 1982; de la Breteche-Tenenbaum) is still unchecked. Tenenbaum-Wu 2003 does not cover mu, because it requires kappa>0.  The novelty assessment is fair. The line-one L^2 packaging (DC3 constant, DC4 profile, DC5 rate) may be new as stated, but no exhaustive search was done. The OPEN_CUTS section 6 target is RH-equivalent per sigma0 and is not a bounded problem.

**Missed prior art flagged:**
- S. M. Gonek, Trans. AMS 364 (2012), arXiv:0704.3448, section on zeta_X*: defines the pole-corrected finite Euler product P*_X(s)=P_X(s)exp(-F2((s-1)log X)). It keeps the pole factor exactly for X large relative to t. This is the closest literature analogue of P_X/C_X, with a Selberg-weighted cutoff and F2=2E2(2z)-E2(z) in place of the sharp E1.
- A. Hildebrand, G. Tenenbaum, JTNB 5 (1993), Lemma 2.5 (p. 426): u rho(u)=int_{u-1}^u rho, rho>0, rho'<0, rho<=1/Gamma(u+1). The proof (first-zero contradiction, then induction) is essentially the 'elementary proof' in PROOF.md DC5.
- Hildebrand-Tenenbaum 1993, Lemma 3.4 with region (3.13) verified from the scan: 1-(log y)^{-2/5-eps}<=sigma<=2, \|tau\|<=exp((log y)^{3/5-eps}). This pins kappa=2/5+eps for the moving-region extension proposed in T1.
- O. Gorodetsky, arXiv:2212.01949, Corollary 2.9 and Lemma 2.8: complex-s versions (\|Im s\|<=1; or min\|rho-s-t\|>>1) of the unconditional bound L(y)^{-c}y^{1-Re s} and the RH bound y^{1/2-Re s}log y for log G1. With Gorodetsky arXiv:2211.08973 Corollary 5.4 (complex s, zeta(s+t)!=0 for t>=0), these answer the draft's question about extending DC6's converse to complex s.
- H. Bohr, R. Courant, J. reine angew. Math. 144 (1914) 249-274 (zbMATH 45.0718.02): already the remainder R_N=zeta/F_N of the Euler product on 1/2<sigma<=1, shown close to 1 for almost all t. This is the historical source for the vertical-average route T6 and for the non-uniformity in t in O5.
- G. Tenenbaum, J. Wu, 'Moyennes de certaines fonctions multiplicatives sur les entiers friables', J. reine angew. Math. 564 (2003) 119-166 (zbMATH 1195.11132): the general framework for sums of multiplicative functions over friable integers. Its hypothesis kappa>0 excludes mu (kappa=-1), which is relevant to (and a negative check for) the novelty question for the DC4 pointwise analogue M(x,y) ~ c x omega'(u)/(log y)^2. Alladi 1982 (J. Number Theory 14, 86-98; Trans. AMS 272, 87-105) is bibliographically confirmed but its content was not checked.

### Known obstructions

- **BRG-FAMILIES-O1** A functional equation without an Euler product does not imply RH: Davenport-Heilbronn functions, Epstein zetas with class number >1, and general linear combinations have off-line zeros, about T of them in every strip 1/2<sigma1<sigma2<1. — *Davenport-Heilbronn, J. London Math. Soc. 11 (1936); Voronin; Y. Lee arXiv:1204.6297 Thms 1.1-1.3; Saias-Weingartner arXiv:0807.0783*. How it constrains: Any 'Riemann structure' or generalized object (#763/#764) built only from completion, functional equation, positivity of a theta-type source and Mellin observation cannot force RH. The repository's theta-quotient real zeros and Epstein zeros are instances. Multiplicativity (Euler product) has to be a load-bearing input in any RH-facing transfer. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-FAMILIES-O2** Statistical or 'almost all' RH is compatible with infinitely many off-line zeros: Bombieri-Hejhal give 100% of zeros simple and on the line for linear combinations (under GRH+H0), while Voronin, Lee and Saias-Weingartner give about T off-line zeros. — *Bombieri-Hejhal, Duke 80 (1995) 821-862 (zbMATH 0853.11074); Perelli survey Thm 7.4*. How it constrains: Family or density-one conclusions cannot certify an individual member. This matches the #740 rejection rule 'replaces an individual conclusion by a density-one statement'. [secondary_source; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-FAMILIES-O3** Large sieve / orthogonality: the family second moment is dominated by its diagonal, so isolating the principal member by positivity costs a factor equal to the family size and gives only the trivial bound. Bombieri-Vinogradov ('GRH on average') never touches the principal character, which needs the zeta zero-free region. — *Davenport, Multiplicative Number Theory, Chs. 27-28; Iwaniec-Kowalski, Ch. 7 and Thm 17.1*. How it constrains: Any family estimate proved by orthogonality (e.g. R-106071.2) gives, for the principal channel L(s,chi_0)=zeta(s)(1-ell^{-s}), at most what was put in. A subpower principal-member extraction is RH-strength (charter #736 outcome 3). [memory_unverified; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-FAMILIES-O4** Threshold barrier: family nonvanishing is proved at 1/2-eps, but 1/2+eps is needed to individualize (exclude Landau-Siegel zeros). A mean over the level aspect does not give the per-level consequence. — *Iwaniec-Sarnak, Israel J. Math. 120 (2000) (zbMATH 0992.11037); Katz-Sarnak Bull. AMS 36 (1999) eq. (68)*. How it constrains: Even successful family-to-member mechanisms sit exactly at a critical exponent, as the repository's critical-versus-supercritical (SHARP) dichotomy does. Expect the principal extraction to fail precisely at the critical scale. [secondary_source; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-FAMILIES-O5** 1-level density positivity needs GRH or a 'real-or-on-line' hypothesis: a complex off-line zero contributes phi((beta-1/2+i gamma)...) of indefinite sign to a nonnegative test function. — *Iwaniec-Luo-Sarnak, Publ. IHES 91 (2000), §1 (GRH assumption, p.57) and §10 (Cor 10.4 assumes zeros real or on the line)*. How it constrains: In #740 candidate C ('amplified positive quadratic form'), positivity of the off-line contribution has to be proved, not assumed. Otherwise the argument assumes the conclusion for nonprincipal members, which #740 lists as an automatic rejection. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-FAMILIES-O6** Deuring-Heilbronn: the natural propagation of a hypothetical bad zero through a family is repulsive. It strengthens nonvanishing (lower bounds for L(1,chi)) for the other members instead of producing forbidden mass. — *J. Stopple arXiv:1201.0713 (Theorem); Benli-Goel-Twiss-Zaman arXiv:2410.06082; Heilbronn 1934*. How it constrains: #740's 'one bad zeta zero -> family statistic -> contradiction' must target a statistic for which the propagated effect is provably forbidden. The classical propagation leads to true statements (Heilbronn's class number theorem), not contradictions. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-FAMILIES-O7** Density theorems require essential orthogonality: Kowalski-Michel/Montgomery-type bounds sum_f N(f,alpha,T) << \|F\|^{c(1-alpha)} hold for families with a large sieve. Families whose members share zeta as a factor (Rankin-Selberg L(f x f-bar)=zeta L(Ad f)) carry a common principal component and admit no such bound. — *Kowalski-Michel arXiv:math/9707238 Thm 1.1 (verified); the non-orthogonality remark is my synthesis*. How it constrains: Propagating a zeta zero to many members of an ORTHOGONAL family, enough to contradict a density theorem, is exactly what orthogonality prevents. This is R-108450's principal-mode firewall in classical language. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-FAMILIES-O8** Selberg-class degree rigidity: S_1 is {zeta, L(s+i theta,chi)}, and S^#_d is empty for 0<d<1 and for 1<d<2. — *Kaczorowski-Perelli, Acta Math. 182 (1999); Ann. of Math. 173 (2011) 1397-1441; Perelli survey Thms 6.3-6.4*. How it constrains: Generalized L-object deformations (#764) cannot interpolate degree within Selberg-type axioms below 2. Continuous families must leave the axiom package, e.g. by losing the Euler product, and then OB01 applies. [secondary_source; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-FAMILIES-O9** Over function fields, RH for every member and family equidistribution come from cohomology and monodromy (Deligne Weil I, Thm 3.2: rational local factors, symplectic big monodromy, positivity of 2k-th tensor powers, a radius-of-convergence lemma). Katz-Sarnak equidistribution needs q->infinity first. — *P. Deligne, 'La conjecture de Weil I', Publ. IHES 43 (1974) 273-307, Thm 3.2 and Lemmas 3.3-3.6 ('catalysé par la lecture de Rankin'); Katz-Sarnak Bull. AMS 36 §3-4*. How it constrains: The function-field mirror (#737) identifies zeros with local Frobenius eigenvalues on finite-dimensional cohomology. The number-field analogue of the same Rankin-squaring argument controls Satake parameters (Ramanujan-type), not zeros of zeta. 'Purity transfers to principal extraction' therefore has no known mechanism over Q. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-FAMILIES-O10** For quadratic twists, the minimalist target avg e_d^2=o(1) implies the analytic Goldfeld conjecture (open). Gouvea-Mazur give >= X^{1/2} even-sign twists with a central zero, so e_d>=2 infinitely often. — *Katz-Sarnak Bull. AMS 36 §5; Smith arXiv:2503.17619; Heath-Brown arXiv:math/0305114*. How it constrains: TWIST-EXCESS-RANK-L2 in its o(1) form is at least as hard as a named open conjecture. The bounded form is known only under GRH, through explicit-formula moment methods. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]

### Transplantable tools

- **BRG-FAMILIES-T1** Explicit conductor-uniform Weil majorant for complex powers of L(T,chi): \|[T^n]L(T,chi)^z\| <= q^{n/2} binom(n+Am,n) for \|z\|<=A, deg(modulus)=m (*Afshar-Porritt arXiv:1709.01963, Prop. 3.1 and Thm 2*). Target gap: B-027 hold: uniform conductor dependence of L-106213 (stopped rough shells, growing conductors). Next step: (1) Replace C_{chi,q}(n+1)^{r/2} at tower level m=1 by the explicit binom(n+m/2-3/2,n)q^{n/2}. (2) Bound the levels k>=2 by the majorant prod_k(1-sqrt(q)\|u\|^k)^{-\|gamma_k\| deg l}, plus the explicit (1-qu^2)^{-1/8} principal factor for quadratic chi, to get C(q)^{deg l}. (3) Write the U-rough stopped shell as the complete tower times the finite correction prod_{deg P<U}(1-chi(P)u^{deg P}/2)^{-1}, and bound that correction on \|u\|=q^{-1/2} (about exp(O(q^{U/2}/U))). This states the admissible (U, deg l, n) range explicitly.. Risk: The finite rough-correction factor may be exponentially large unless U <= (2-eps)log_q n. That would show the stopped-shell transfer is affordable only for very small U. In any case it says nothing about the number-field principal member.
- **BRG-FAMILIES-T2** Deligne's Rankin-squaring / tensor-power argument: positive-coefficient local factors of 2k-th tensor powers + exact abscissa from invariant theory + Landau-type radius-of-convergence lemma; the loss (family size) enters with exponent 1/(2k) (*Deligne, Publ. IHES 43 (1974), §3, Thm 3.2, Lemmas 3.3-3.6*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS §5: principal-member extraction at subpower cost. Next step: Test whether some repository family has a positive-coefficient 2k-th moment Dirichlet series whose abscissa is computable exactly, independently of zeros, the analogue of H^2_c of the tensor power. If so, the existing Mellin-Landau consumer (CURRENT_RESULTS §3, MATHEMATICS.md §1) can play Lemmas 3.5-3.6, and the family-size loss disappears as k -> infinity.. Risk: High. Over Q, computing that abscissa for Mobius-weighted sources is RH-strength. The argument naturally bounds local parameters (Ramanujan-type), not zeros of zeta (OB09).
- **BRG-FAMILIES-T3** ILS positivity extraction: extended-support 1-level density with all terms nonnegative; drop all but one term (*Iwaniec-Luo-Sarnak, Publ. IHES 91 (2000) §10, Cor. 10.4*). Target gap: #740 'family propagation/amplification theorem' and OPEN_CUTS §5. Next step: For the Dirichlet family lift L-106000, write the explicit-formula family statistic with a Fejer-type test function. Compute the sign of the contribution of an off-line zero beta+i gamma, uniformly in beta. Identify the smallest extra hypothesis that keeps it nonnegative; this is the analogue of ILS's 'real-or-on-line' assumption.. Risk: ILS needed both a non-family exponential-sum hypothesis (Hypothesis S) and reality of off-line zeros. Removing the latter is the open problem itself (OB05).
- **BRG-FAMILIES-T4** Large-sieve zero-density theorems for essentially orthogonal families (no point is a common zero of more than \|F\|^{3(1-alpha)/(2-alpha)+eps} members) (*Kowalski-Michel arXiv:math/9707238 Thm 1.1; Montgomery, Topics in Multiplicative Number Theory (LNM 227) Ch. 12*). Target gap: #740 'unconditional family upper bound' and 'contradiction' step. Next step: In the #740 ledger, fill the UNCONDITIONAL FAMILY UPPER BOUND slot with the Kowalski-Michel bound. The open slot is then explicitly: find an essentially orthogonal family in which a hypothetical zeta zero forces zeros (not just detector poles) in more than \|F\|^{3(1-beta)/(2-beta)} members.. Risk: A shared zeta factor destroys orthogonality (OB07), and Deuring-Heilbronn suggests the induced effect is repulsive (OB06). This may become a no-go theorem, which the charter also accepts as an outcome.
- **BRG-FAMILIES-T5** Tate isogeny theorem + Honda-Tate + Howe-Nart-Ritzenthaler classification of genus-2 Weil polynomials (*Tate, Invent. Math. 2 (1966); HNR arXiv:math/0607515 Thm 1.2, Tables 1-2*). Target gap: B-031 hold (no isogeny/polarization/Jacobian statement from the +q factor predicate) and the q=7 / 251-atom census replay. Next step: (1) Replace the polynomial predicate by the HNR table criteria for split and simple classes. (2) Record that (q,a,b)=(5,0,-10) is realized by y^2=x^5-x, a simple supersingular class over F_5 (exhaustive check script: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/fam/code/g2f5.py). (3) Cross-check the 251-atom incidence against HNR's predicted occurrence set.. Risk: Low mathematically. The family convention (y^2=prod(a_i-z), the AGL quotient, stack versus coarse measures) must be matched before counting.
- **BRG-FAMILIES-T6** Ball arithmetic with rigorous complex Gamma and upper incomplete Gamma (Arb/FLINT: acb_hypgeom_gamma_upper) (*F. Johansson, arXiv:1611.02831 (Arb); arXiv:1606.06977 (rigorous hypergeometric and incomplete gamma)*). Target gap: B-011 hold: nearest-rounded mp.sqrt, out-of-context remainder multiplication and 25-digit endpoint shortening in the Epstein winding certificate. Next step: Re-evaluate Lambda(s) = 1/(s-1) - 1/s + sum_v [(pi Q)^{-s}Gamma(s,pi Q) + (pi Q)^{s-1}Gamma(1-s,pi Q)] in acb with outward rounding throughout, including the lattice-tail and Cauchy sup bounds. Then replay the 3696-step walk and the edge combination.. Risk: None to RH. The certified instance only locates a zero of a phenomenon that is already classical (PA08); its value is evidential and for calibration.
- **BRG-FAMILIES-T7** Hoffstein-Lockhart + Goldfeld-Hoffstein-Lieman effective zero-free region for adjoint (sym^2) L-functions (*Ann. of Math. 140 (1994) 161-181 (zbMATH 0814.11032)*). Target gap: B-017: 'independent automorphic normalization/import check still required' for the near-one symmetric-square comparison. Next step: Cite GHL for the absence of an exceptional zero and for the c/log k zero-free region of L(s, sym^2 f) for level-one holomorphic f. Then apply B's repaired sigma_0=1+1/log k comparison verbatim.. Risk: Normalization mismatch: Maass versus holomorphic, analytic conductor in k, and the log(lambda N) versus log k scale.
- **BRG-FAMILIES-T8** Saias-Weingartner orthogonal decomposition H_q = direct sum of E_{q,psi} (periodic Dirichlet series) as a counterfeit detector (*Saias-Weingartner arXiv:0807.0783, Thms 1-2*). Target gap: #763 Q8 / #764 'counterfeit generator': which axioms force genuine L-functions. Next step: For periodic or character-combination generalized objects in the atlas, compute the E_{q,psi} components. More than one nonzero primitive component predicts about T zeros in 1/2<sigma<1+eta. Use this as an exact, theorem-backed counterfeit rejection test instead of contour searches.. Risk: Applies only to periodic coefficients (GL(1)). Higher-degree analogues are known only in partial forms (Kaczorowski-Kulas, Bombieri-Hejhal).

### Novelty assessment

Classical, or classical in substance (cite rather than re-derive): - **Euler restoration (L-106000.8/.10):** textbook identity for the principal character. - **Witt identity (L-106212.3):** exactly the Moreau / Metropolis-Rota cyclotomic necklace identity at k=1/2. I verified it to degree 16. - **Witt tower of L-functions (L-106212.4):** a known technique for Euler products (Witt 1937; Moree; Ramaré 2019). The function-field resonance bookkeeping is a straightforward specialization. - **m=1 coefficient majorant of L-106213:** coincides with Afshar-Porritt Prop. 3.1 at z=-1/2, which is already uniform in the conductor. The rest is a function-field Selberg-Delange argument. - **Orbit/stabilizer measures, the Lambda^2 Sym^3 plethysm, the GL2 Loewner central-atom signs, the HPL sign convention (Crainic) and the Chow/Segre imports (Rubei; Raicu-Sam-Weyman):** standard. - **R-108450 and R-106071:** correct, finite restatements of classical orthogonality and homogeneity facts. They are well placed as firewalls but are not new obstructions.  New in context but classical mechanisms: - **Proper theta-quotient real-zero corollary:** a quotient-minimized version of the Chowla-Selberg / Bateman-Grosswald mechanism applied to a Rankin-Selberg/Epstein average. The 'minimum over the quotient fibre at each t' twist appears new as far as I could find. It is consistent with GRH: for Hecke eigenforms the Petersson norm is about k times larger, so the criterion fails (my memory-based estimate). - **Epstein winding certificate (held):** locates a specific zero of a classical phenomenon. The point z* has a non-fundamental scaled discriminant, so Voronin and Lee do not literally apply, but the off-line behavior is expected. - **Alternant closed form for Hadamard powers:** the repository already positions it as a new-in-context closed form with a classical technique. Its generic degree law and backward deficit match the a-invariant of Segre products (Stanley reciprocity; Goto-Watanabe). I checked this only from memory.  Possibly genuinely new, low confidence (I did not check the full sources): - the marked Chow witness and exactness promotion; - the torsion-entry and crowding results; - the reciprocal-slope multiplicity lemma; - the specific source-typed family detector dictionary.  The open cut: - **Principal-member transfer (OPEN_CUTS §5) for the Dirichlet family is at least RH-strength.** By L-106000.8 the principal member is zeta times a finite factor, so any subpower principal extraction from a family moment yields a GRH-type estimate for the principal channel. The literature has only partial templates: ILS quasi-RH under Hypothesis S plus real off-line zeros; the Iwaniec-Sarnak 1/2+eps threshold for Siegel zeros; Deligne's tensor-power trick, which works only cohomologically over F_q. Each needs an extra ingredient with no known number-field analogue for zeta's zeros. - **TWIST-EXCESS-RANK-L2:** its o(1) form is at least as strong as the analytic Goldfeld conjecture; its bounded form is known only under GRH. - **The smaller burdens** (Epstein certificate, Chow witness replay, Poincaré PF40 precision) are bounded, legitimate tasks. I found no literature that discharges PF40: the repository already uses the Petersson/Bessel Gram estimate (PF4), and the open cross term concerns the period operator.  I found no explicit witness that any repository statement in this programme is wrong.

### Recommended actions

- Cite Moreau (1872) and Metropolis-Rota (Adv. Math. 50, 1983) for L-106212.3, and restate it as 'the cyclotomic necklace identity at alpha=1/2'. Cite Witt (1937), Moree (2000) and Ramaré (arXiv:1911.10991, Thm 2 / Lemma 11) for the Euler-product-to-L-tower technique behind L-106212.4.
- Cite Afshar-Porritt (arXiv:1709.01963, Prop. 3.1) for L-106213.2. Use its explicit binom(n+Am,n)q^{n/2} conductor dependence to reduce the B-027 hold to two remaining items: the m>=2 tower levels and the finite U-rough Euler correction. State the admissible (U, deg l, n) range explicitly (TT01).
- Record in OPEN_CUTS §5 that, for the Dirichlet family mod ell, subpower principal-member extraction is RH-strength, because L-106000.8 makes the principal member zeta(s)(1-67^{-s}). This is charter #736 outcome 3; compare 'Lindelöf on average does not imply individual Lindelöf' and the Iwaniec-Sarnak 50% barrier.
- Add ILS §10 Cor. 10.4, Iwaniec-Sarnak (Israel J. Math. 2000) and Deligne Weil I §3 to the #736/#740 amplifier audit as the three known family-to-member templates. For each, list the extra hypothesis it needs: Hypothesis S plus real off-line zeros; the 1/2+eps proportion; cohomological monodromy.
- For #740, put Deuring-Heilbronn (Stopple arXiv:1201.0713; Benli-Goel-Twiss-Zaman arXiv:2410.06082) and Goldfeld (1976) in the 'family theorem map' as existing propagation theorems whose conclusions are true, hence non-contradictory. Use Kowalski-Michel (arXiv:math/9707238, Thm 1.1) as the unconditional family upper bound. State the remaining slot as: force zeros of an essentially orthogonal family from a zeta zero (TT04).
- For B-031, replace 'no isogeny statement accepted' with the precise imports Tate (1966), Honda-Tate and Howe-Nart-Ritzenthaler Thm 1.2. Add the observation that the (5,0,-10) ghost is realized in the declared monic-squarefree-quintic family by y^2=x^5-x over F_5, a simple supersingular isogeny class. Before integrating, a contributor should independently replay the exhaustive F_5 check.
- For the theta-quotient corollary (B-043), cite Chowla-Selberg (1949/1967) and Bateman-Grosswald (1964, Thm 3) as the classical prototype. Record the Rankin-Selberg/Epstein-average interpretation of the source. Optionally add the eigenform consistency check: R_f -> 0 for Hecke eigenforms, so the corollary does not touch GRH for sym^2.
- For the Epstein certificate (B-011), cite Davenport-Heilbronn (1936), Voronin, Lee (arXiv:1204.6297) and Bombieri-Mueller for the phenomenon. Note the non-fundamental-discriminant gap for z*. Re-certify with Arb ball arithmetic (TT06) rather than patching mpmath rounding.
- For TWIST-EXCESS-RANK-L2, add to the GL2 README that avg e_d^2=o(1) implies the analytic Goldfeld conjecture (which follows from BSD by Smith, arXiv:2503.17619). Heath-Brown (2004, Thm 3) gives average rank <= 3/2 only under GRH. Present the target as a conditional or open moment, not a near-term arithmetic gate.
- For B-017, cite Hoffstein-Lockhart with the Goldfeld-Hoffstein-Lieman appendix (Ann. Math. 140, 1994) for the sym^2 zero-free region without exceptional zero, and pin the normalization.
- For #763/#764 counterfeit rejection, adopt Saias-Weingartner (periodic coefficients), Kaczorowski-Perelli (S_1 classification; S^#_d empty for 1<d<2) and Bombieri-Hejhal (100% on-line zeros compatible with off-line zeros) as theorem-backed benchmarks, and cite them in the atlas firewalls.
- For the alternant / backward-deficit results (T-108510/T-108522), check the correspondence with Stanley reciprocity and the Goto-Watanabe a-invariant a = -max d_i of Segre products. If it holds, cite it; it explains both the generic degree prod d - max d and the fixed deficit d, and gives a clean criterion for when specialization drops the degree.
- For the factor67 and direct_main OPEN_RH_EQUIVALENT gates, add a pointer to the classical Littlewood and Landau one-sided Mertens equivalences (Montgomery-Vaughan §15.1). This makes explicit that these are smoothed one-sided M(x) bounds and that their family lifts are GRH statements member by member.

### Independent verifier

The draft is careful. I checked almost every citation against full text or zbMATH; almost all exist and are quoted accurately. I found no statement in the programme that is shown to be wrong. The main problems are miscalibrated relations and one understated prior result.  (1) PA04 / TT01 / recommended action 2 / novelty assessment. Afshar-Porritt Prop. 3.3 already bounds exactly the repository's H_{chi,n} (z=-1/2), with constants uniform in q and conductor degree. Their polynomial factor binom(n+Am,n)n^{c_A} is weaker than the repository's (n+1)^{r/2+2} at fixed chi. So the 'conductor-uniform complete shell' step is prior art. Only the U-rough stopped shells and masks of B-027 remain open.  (2) PA06 calls R-108450 an 'equivalent reformulation' of Bombieri-Vinogradov. It is only an illustrative Parseval analogue; the draft's own notes say so.  (3) PA21 contains a false sub-claim. One-sided Landau equivalences do not transfer to L(s,chi): real chi runs into the Siegel-zero obstruction, and for complex chi a one-sided bound is meaningless. The draft also wrongly groups T91005 (a Cauchy-recurrence criterion) and CJHI (an operator intertwiner) with the negative-mass gates.  (4) PA10/OB06 mischaracterize Benli-Goel-Twiss-Zaman. It is Siegel-zero-driven repulsion, not an explicit version of Stopple's Deuring-direction theorem.  (5) Smaller fixes: - Moreau's pages are 309-314. - Gouvea-Mazur's bound is X^{1/2-epsilon}. - The 'bounded second moment under GRH' for twist families is unsupported. - Davenport-Heilbronn's hypotheses, as reviewed, do not require a fundamental discriminant, so the z* coverage caveat applies only to the strip theorems. - PA18's 'repo_weaker' label is wrong: Hoffstein-Lockhart/GHL partly supply the import. GHL covers only the real exceptional zero, and Hoffstein-Lockhart is stated for Maass forms. - PA19: HNR do not settle principal polarizability. - PA14: the Deligne remark is in Katz-Sarnak section 4. - OB07: 'admit no bound' should be 'admit no unconditional proof without RH-type input'. - OB01: 'general linear combinations' is overbroad.  (6) The most important missed prior art is Lagarias-Suzuki (2006). It studies exactly integrals of E*(z,s) against nonnegative measures. Its constant-term threshold 4 pi e^{-gamma} = 7.0555, which produces two real off-line zeros, is the closest classical analogue of the theta-quotient real-zero corollary, closer than Chowla-Selberg. It also shows that positivity can sometimes force RH (for example Weng's rank-2 zeta), which tempers OB01.  (7) The B-031 ghost realization (y^2=x^5-x over F_5 gives (1-5T^2)^2) is new information beyond the repository. I confirmed it with an independent exhaustive script: 2500 monic squarefree quintics, exactly one hit. It should still be replayed by a contributor before integration, as the draft says.  Evidence levels: several items marked memory_unverified or secondary are now verified (PA13, PA18 at zbMATH level, PA07 via Ribeiro-Yakubovich). PA03, PA05, PA20 and OB03 rely partly on direct mathematical checks or memory rather than theorem-number verification.  Scratch scripts are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/verif_fam/ (g2f5_indep.py, zb.py). No repository file was modified.

**Missed prior art flagged:**
- Afshar-Porritt, arXiv:1709.01963 (Acta Arith. 187 (2019) 101-124), Proposition 3.3 with Lemma 3.2. Statement: \|sum_{f monic, deg n} mu^2(f) chi(f) z^{omega(f)}\| <= q^{n/2} binom(n+Am,n) n^{c_A} for nonprincipal chi mod d (deg d=m), A>1, \|z\|<=A, with c_A depending only on A. At z=-1/2 this is the complete half-source shell H_{chi,n} of L-106213, already uniform in q and conductor. It covers part of what B-027 holds and what TT01 proposes to build.
- J. C. Lagarias & M. Suzuki, 'The Riemann hypothesis for certain integrals of Eisenstein series', J. Number Theory 118 (2006) 98-122, arXiv:math/0412039. Statement: F_mu(s) = integral of E*(z,s) against a nonnegative measure mu satisfies RH for several natural mu. The constant term a_0(y,s) satisfies RH except for exactly two real zeros when y > 4 pi e^{-gamma} = 7.0555, which is Bateman-Grosswald's threshold. This is the closest prior art for the proper theta-source real-zero corollary (B-043): for fixed f, that source is an integral of E*(z,s) against y^k\|f\|^2 dmu concentrated at height about k/(4 pi j).
- H. Ki, 'Zeros of the constant term in the Chowla-Selberg formula', Acta Arith. 124 (2006) 197-204 (zbMATH 1155.11047): all non-real zeros of the Eisenstein constant term are simple and on the critical line. Also W. Müller, arXiv:math/0703340, for a spectral interpretation. Both are companions to Lagarias-Suzuki for the theta-quotient zero geometry.
- J. P. Keating & Z. Rudnick, 'Squarefree polynomials and Möbius values in short intervals and arithmetic progressions', Algebra Number Theory 10 (2016) 375-420 (zbMATH 1391.11156). Statement: mean and variance of mu and mu^2 in arithmetic progressions over F_q[t] with growing modulus, in the q->infinity limit. Relevant to the B-027 growing-conductor regime and to the fixed-q versus large-q distinction (PA14).
- M. Rosen, Number Theory in Function Fields (GTM 210), Proposition 4.3, as cited by Afshar-Porritt: for nonprincipal chi mod m, L(u,chi) is a polynomial of degree <= deg m - 1. This is the direct source of L-106213.1 (r <= deg l - 1), together with Weil's theorem.
- Howe, Maisner, Nart & Ritzenthaler, 'Principally polarizable isogeny classes of abelian surfaces over finite fields' (Math. Res. Lett. 2008, from memory), and Maisner & Nart (Exp. Math. 2002; HNR's [MN02]). These classify principal polarizability separately from Jacobian membership, which is relevant to the B-031 'no principal-polarization statement' boundary.
- Classical Euler-product-to-zeta(ms) expansions for constants such as Artin's constant: J. W. Wrench, Math. Comp. 15 (1961); H. Cohen, 'High precision computation of Hardy-Littlewood constants' (preprint). These are the same necklace-type device as L-106212.4 (memory; not re-checked).
- Concavity and averaging of the Schur complement or shorted operator: W. N. Anderson & G. E. Trapp, 'Shorted operators II' (SIAM J. Appl. Math. 1975), and T. Ando's concavity results (memory). These are the classical form of the B-report section 6 quotient-energy identities H_Q=(pi H^{-1} pi*)^{-1} and Schur(integral of H) >= integral of Schur(H) used by the theta-quotient sources.
- A. E. Ingham, 'On two conjectures in the theory of numbers', Amer. J. Math. 64 (1942), from memory. One-sided boundedness of M(x)/sqrt(x) would force RH and linear relations among zeros. This is a sharper classical comparator for one-sided 'negative-mass' gates than Littlewood alone (PA21).


## MELLIN_WAVELET

**Programme scope examined:** MELLIN_WAVELET: fixed Mellin detector, negative mass and harmonic analysis of the critical signed source. Covers CURRENT_RESULTS.md sections 3, 4 and 5 and the OPEN_CUTS.md fixed-detector section. Packets read: research/integrated/mellin_landau, wavelet_xd, q4, vaughan_half_divisor and carry_hinge, plus their linked proofs (reviews/C/pass4-math-completion/proofs/MELLIN_ANALYTIC_GAPS.md M1-M8; revi
