### Known obstructions

- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O1** Ingham's linear-relations theorem. If M(x)/sqrt x or L(x)/sqrt x is bounded on one side, then RH and simple zeros hold, and the positive ordinates satisfy infinitely many integer linear relations. Under LI, liminf x^{-1/2}M(x) = -inf and limsup = +inf. — *A. E. Ingham, 'On two conjectures in the theory of numbers', Amer. J. Math. 64 (1942) 313-319 (zbMATH 0063.02974 review). Restated in Mossinghoff-Trudgian arXiv:2007.14537 section 1.*. How it constrains: It rules out, under LI, pointwise eventual sign for 'Pólya-class' critical detectors: those with only a constant main term and zero coefficients r_gamma ~ 1/(gamma zeta'(rho)) that are not absolutely summable. The repo's SHARP critical H^{[1]} with jump kernel T(y)=4sqrt y-3 is in this class. Its Mellin transform B(s+1/2)(4/(s-1/2)-3/s) gives bias -3B(1/2)=1.80332 and r_gamma ~ (1-67^{-rho})/(i gamma zeta'(rho)). Analyst computation: sum over the first 200 zeros of 2\|r_gamma\| is 1.426 and still growing, tracking the Mertens analogue (1.393). Under RH plus Gonek's second-moment asymptotic, Hölder gives sum 1/\|rho zeta'(rho)\| = inf. So 'H^{[1]}(x)>0 for all large x' would force linear relations among zeta ordinates. Only the negative-mass/weighted-variation form (OPEN.ARITH.CV) is viable. The obstruction does NOT apply to detectors with log drift (rows 2,3, W_X) or with absolutely summable gamma^{-2} coefficients and a large bias (G_1 with S=4(sqrt y-1)_+: bias 2.40443 vs sum 2\|r\| ~ 0.066 over 200 zeros; C_m). [abstract_only (zbMATH review) + secondary_source (Mossinghoff-Trudgian intro). The application to H^{[1]} is analyst derivation plus non-directed mpmath numerics and is not reviewed.; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O2** Pólya-, Turán- and Mertens-type fixed sign conjectures, each implying RH through a Landau consumer, are false. — *C. B. Haselgrove, Mathematika 5 (1958) 141-145 (Zbl 0085.27102). Odlyzko-te Riele, J. reine angew. Math. 357 (1985). Tanaka, Tokyo J. Math. 3 (1980). Borwein-Ferguson-Mossinghoff, Math. Comp. 77 (2008). Kim-Nguyen, arXiv:2502.21021 (first Mertens counterexample below exp(1.96e19)). Humphries, arXiv:1108.1524 (under RH+LI, positive log density of Pólya counterexamples).*. How it constrains: Positivity observed up to large X, such as the repo's row scans or historical L(x)<=0 up to 9e8, is no evidence for a fixed detector's eventual sign when it lies in the Pólya class. The repo's preference for the subpower negative-mass premise over pointwise sign is consistent with this history. [abstract_only / secondary_source; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O3** The negative-mass premise is RH-equivalent for every fixed detector in the repo's class, so OPEN_CUTS section 1 cannot be weaker than RH. Graded form: for theta in (0,1/2), int_1^Y F_-dx/x << Y^{theta+eps} for a fixed noncancelling detector excludes zeros with Re rho > 1/2+theta. Conversely, a zero-free half-plane Re s>1/2+theta gives \|F(X)\| << X^{theta+eps} for 1/s^2-smoothed rows, via Titchmarsh 14.2-type reciprocal bounds and Mellin inversion. — *Littlewood 1912. Titchmarsh sections 14.2 and 14.25. Landau / MV Lemma 15.1. Repo R17 (reviews/D-final/REPAIRS.md) and R15 part 1 for the reciprocal bound.*. How it constrains: Any unconditional partial result with theta<1/2 would be a zeta zero-free strip, which no one has ever established (the best known regions are of Vinogradov-Korobov type). Partial 'Y^theta negative mass' results in the Q4, half-divisor, XD or wavelet coordinates are therefore at least as hard as quasi-RH. [memory_unverified (classical); the graded equivalence is an analyst derivation to be reviewed; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O4** Sign-change counts are Theta(log Y) for no-main-term detectors. Kaczorowski-Pintz show V(f,Y) >= c log Y whenever the Mellin transform has a nonreal boundary singularity but is regular on the real axis. Under RH the logarithmic horizon caps the count at O(log Y). — *Kaczorowski-Pintz 1986/87 (Zbl 0613.10036, 0631.10026). Morrill-Platt-Trudgian arXiv:1910.14203. Grześkowiak et al. arXiv:2408.10399.*. How it constrains: This supports the OPEN_CUTS firewall: counting negative intervals of the wavelet or XD detectors cannot distinguish RH from not-RH. L(Y) <= log Y is automatic, as R7 already notes. [abstract_only; verifier: relation_overstated]
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O5** Finite truncations of L^2 RH criteria have unconditional logarithmic defects: for N-term Nyman-Beurling approximants, \|\|F-chi\|\| >= C/sqrt(log N), with Burnol's sharper constant. — *Báez-Duarte arXiv:math/0202141 eq. (1.2), citing Balazard-Saias and Burnol (Adv. Math. 170 (2002)).*. How it constrains: Any fixed-cutoff or finite-window positive or energy certificate in the wavelet suffix-field Hilbert space can approach a criterion at best logarithmically. This is consistent with the repo's REFUTATION.WAVELET.CRITICAL_DESMOOTHING and finite-scope firewalls. [fulltext_read (as quoted by Báez-Duarte); verifier: relation_overstated]
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O6** The Landau-Pintz finite-to-zero-free method cannot improve knowledge for zeta. Johnston-Trudgian call it 'doomed to fail' for zeta, because RH is verified to height 3e12 and explicit zero-free regions are already strong. — *D. R. Johnston, T. Trudgian, 'A round of Pintz to celebrate oscillations in sums', arXiv:2511.09978 (v4 2026), section 1. Pintz, Math. Pannonica 28 (2022) 58-64 (Zbl 1513.11159).*. How it constrains: A finite negative-mass computation for rows 2,3, W_X or C_m, converted by an explicit Landau-Pintz theorem, yields only zero-free information weaker than existing verification. It cannot serve as evidence toward the subpower premise. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O7** Family averages of sign criteria can be proved unconditionally while the principal member stays RH-hard. Suzuki proves unconditional negativity of character-averaged weighted Chebyshev Riesz sums for Q >= x, while the individual sign is equivalent to (G)RH. — *M. Suzuki arXiv:2411.07436, Thm 7 versus Thms 1 and 3.*. How it constrains: This reinforces the repo's principal-member firewall (OPEN_CUTS section 5): a family-averaged negative-mass bound does not supply the fixed principal detector premise. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O8** Selberg's parity phenomenon: sieve or positive-weight (Type I) information alone cannot produce cancellation in mu or lambda sums. — *A. Selberg, Proc. Sympos. Pure Math. 20 (1971). Friedlander-Iwaniec, Opera de Cribro (2010).*. How it constrains: This is a heuristic counterpart of the repo's positive-floor-kernel, positive-divisor-compiler and source-blind-convexity refutations. Positive kernels or positive inverses cannot by themselves yield the signed critical estimate. Genuine Type II (bilinear) signed input is needed, as in the HCNC, XD and UOSACF gates. [memory_unverified; verifier: confirmed]

### Transplantable tools

- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-T1** An explicit-formula converse for log-Riesz detectors with drift (Han 2026, Thm 1.2 method; Suzuki 2024, Thm 1 method). (*S. Han arXiv:2608.27130 section 3. M. Suzuki arXiv:2411.07436 section 3. Titchmarsh section 14.16 (good contours T_nu).*). Target gap: Classify the open nodes OPEN.ARITH.ROWS23_NATIVE and OPEN.ARITH.FIVE_THREE_NEGATIVE_MASS: are they strictly stronger than RH or equivalent to it?. Next step: Compute the double pole at s=0 of C_j/s^2+P_j(s+1/2)/(s^2 zeta(s+1/2)), which gives f_j(X) = kappa_j log X + kappa'_j + Phi_j(log X) + o(1). Here kappa_j = C_j+P_j(1/2)/zeta(1/2), and Phi_j(t)=2Re sum_gamma P_j(rho)e^{i gamma t}/((rho-1/2)^2 zeta'(rho)) under RH and simple zeros. Then verify Han-type truncation with H3' := sum_gamma 1/(gamma^2\|zeta'(1/2+i gamma)\|) < inf. Analyst values: kappa_2=1.11171, kappa_3=0.40646, kappa_W=6.77792, all positive. This makes eventual sign of rows 2,3 and W_X equivalent to RH modulo simple zeros and H3'.. Risk: Gives no unconditional progress. It only removes the possibility that the sign premise is 'stronger than RH' under standard conjectures. The kappa values come from non-directed floating point and must be recomputed with directed arithmetic before canonical use.
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-T2** A graded Landau consumer combined with reciprocal-zeta growth inside a zero-free half-plane. (*Titchmarsh section 14.2 (proof method); the repo's R15 part 1; MV Lemma 15.1.*). Target gap: Calibrate OPEN_CUTS section 1: what does a non-subpower but nontrivial negative-mass bound buy?. Next step: State and prove: for a fixed noncancelling detector, int_1^Y F_- dx/x << Y^{theta+eps} for all eps implies zeta(s) != 0 for Re s > 1/2+theta. For the 1/s^2-smoothed rows, add the converse direction by shifting the Mellin inversion contour to Re s = theta+delta, using \|1/zeta\| << t^eps there. Register it as a separate graded API node.. Risk: No risk to correctness. It makes explicit that any theta<1/2 result would be a zero-free strip, i.e. quasi-RH, so it reframes but does not close the gap.
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-T3** Ingham's Fejér-kernel method plus Haselgrove/Odlyzko-te Riele/Hurst lattice-reduction search. (*Ingham 1942. Haselgrove 1958. Odlyzko-te Riele 1985. G. Hurst, Math. Comp. 87 (2018) (arXiv:1610.08551). Kim-Nguyen arXiv:2502.21021.*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS section 1 'concrete output: a counterexample to a proposed intermediate bound', applied to any proposed pointwise eventual positivity of Pólya-class critical detectors such as SHARP H^{[1]} with T=4sqrt y-3.. Next step: Compute certified r_gamma = (4/(rho-1)-3/(rho-1/2))(1-67^{-rho})/zeta'(rho) for the first 10^4 or more zeros. Use LLL/BKZ to find y with c + 2 Re sum_{gamma<T}(1-gamma/T) r_gamma e^{i gamma y} < 0, where c = -3(1-67^{-1/2})/zeta(1/2) = 1.80332. As in Haselgrove, this would unconditionally refute 'H^{[1]}>=0 eventually', because that hypothesis itself implies RH and simple zeros.. Risk: It needs Fejér-weighted alignment exceeding 1.80, comparable to Hurst's limsup M(x)/sqrt x >= 1.826, so it may need many zeros. It refutes only a pointwise statement that the repo does not currently claim as a target, and leaves OPEN.ARITH.CV untouched.
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-T4** B^2-almost-periodic limiting distributions for explicit-formula functions (Akbary-Ng-Shahabi, Thm 1.2 / Cor 1.3; Rubinstein-Sarnak; Humphries). (*Akbary-Ng-Shahabi arXiv:1306.1657, Thm 1.2 and Cor 1.3. Rubinstein-Sarnak, Exp. Math. 3 (1994). Humphries arXiv:1108.1524.*). Target gap: Predict the size of V(Y) and B_partial(Y) in the corrected excursion estimate (CURRENT_RESULTS section 3) for each fixed detector under RH+LI, and decide which detectors have log-density-zero negative sets.. Next step: For each detector, write phi(y)=F(e^y)-(drift) = c + Re sum r_n e^{i lambda_n y} + E. Check the coefficient conditions of ANS Cor 1.3(a): r_gamma << gamma^{-beta} with beta > 1/2 holds for 1/s^2 rows and the wavelet under \|zeta'(rho)\|^{-1} << \|rho\|^{1/2-eps}. Compute the limiting distribution's mass below 0 to classify detectors into drift, bias or Pólya classes.. Risk: Conditional on RH, LI and negative-moment hypotheses. It is heuristic for the unconditional premise and gives no proof.
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-T5** Explicit Landau-Pintz mean-value Omega theorem. (*Johnston-Trudgian arXiv:2511.09978, Thm 1. Pintz, Math. Pannonica 28 (2022). Pintz, Acta Arith. 42 (1982) and 43 (1984) (Zbl 0469.10019, 0544.10048).*). Target gap: Turn the repo's finite computations (row scans, finite negative-mass certificates) into an honest, quantified implication. This is the 'bounded contribution' type in OPEN_CUTS.. Next step: Instantiate JT Thm 1 with A=f_j, F=P_j(s+1/2), G=s^2 zeta(s+1/2)-type factorization. Output an explicit lower bound for (1/Y) int_1^Y \|f_j - main\| in terms of any hypothetical simple zero beta_0+i gamma_0, then compare with a certified finite scan. Kaczorowski-Pintz one-sided variants would be needed for f_- rather than \|f\|.. Risk: The authors state that for zeta this cannot beat existing RH verification to 3e12. Its value is calibration and firewall, not progress.
- **BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-T6** Gál / Aistleitner-Berkes-Seip GCD-sum bounds, together with Franel-Landau. (*arXiv:1210.0741 (JEMS 2015). Gál 1949.*). Target gap: Q4.JORDAN_PERIODIZED 'positive Gram with squared-logarithmic bound' in the periodized ensemble.. Next step: Rewrite (gcd(d,e)^2-1)/(4de) as (1/3) times the Franel kernel plus a rank-one-type correction. Apply Gál's optimal (log log N)^2 bound for alpha=1 GCD quadratic forms to see whether the periodized log^2 bound improves to (log log)^2.. Risk: Applies only to the periodized model. The repo's own firewall says it does not transfer to the physical fixed-endpoint measure, and it does not touch the signed coarse modes.

### Novelty assessment

Classical, with prior art: - The analytic consumer is classical: M2-M5, the specialized Landau theorem, the holomorphic-defect transfer and the negative-mass relaxation. M3 is Landau's theorem in the Widder Thm II.5b / Montgomery-Vaughan Lemma 15.1 form, proved the standard way. M4 and M7 are routine corollaries of it. The repo's real contributions here are careful bookkeeping: the punctured-germ predicate, the entire-case handling and the zeta(1) normalization gate. These are useful for formalization, but they are not new theorems. - The fixed-row and 5:3 detectors fit a known pattern: first-order logarithmic Riesz means at critical normalization, with a log-drift main term. Suzuki (arXiv:2411.07436, 2024) and Han (arXiv:2608.27130, Aug 2026) prove the same pattern for Lambda and lambda. For the 1/zeta-type rows, the Landau direction is classical, and the converse follows Han's method under simple zeros plus a zeta' summability hypothesis. - The specific numerator algebra appears to be new as specific instances, but it is elementary. This covers the two-row common-zero exclusion -3(a-1)(a-2) and the 5:3 factorization. - Wavelet: the energy identity is finite summation by parts. The spectral-abscissa theorem is a compactly smoothed version of the classical result that the M(x) growth/convergence abscissa is Theta, and its reciprocal-zeta growth step is essentially Titchmarsh section 14.2. - The R4 suffix-field criterion uses Nyman-Beurling-style L^2 dilation duality but is not equivalent to it. - The minimal causal dyadic annihilator (I-sqrt2 S_2)(I-S_2)^2 and its ratio-eight compact wavelet: no prior art was found. This appears new as a construction, but its content is algebraic. - Q4: the mean-value bound is a weaker special case of Montgomery-Vaughan 1974 (already cited). The periodized GCD-square covariance closely resembles Franel-Landau and GCD-sum (Gál) structures. - The Vaughan large-divisor and half-divisor reductions are exact rewrites built on Vaughan's identity and on zeta^{1/2} coefficients (d_{1/2}). - The excursion estimate is Wirtinger's inequality.  Apparently new (with uncertainty): the specific coordinate systems and their exact RH-equivalent or sufficient gates (UOSACF, SACF, HCNC, XD, carry-hinge weighted response, same-K1 translation). No literature equivalents were found. They are reformulations. Each open gate is RH-equivalent or at least zero-free-strip-hard, so none is a weakening.  Observations from this analysis, as yet unreviewed: - The negative-mass branches of OPEN.ARITH.ROWS23_NATIVE, FIVE_THREE and FIXED_DETECTOR are RH-equivalent, not merely sufficient, by the R17/Littlewood converse. - The repo's critical detectors split into three classes:   - drift class: rows 2,3 and W_X, with kappa approximately 1.112, 0.406 and 6.778;   - bias class with absolutely summable gamma^{-2} coefficients: G_1 and C_m;   - Pólya class: SHARP H^{[1]}, with bias 1.803 and coefficients of order 1/(gamma zeta'). Here pointwise eventual positivity conflicts with LI by Ingham 1942.  These numerics are non-directed and finite: an mpmath sum over 200 zeros, and a float scan of the rows to X=10^7 (scripts at /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/mw/rows2.py and /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/mw/coeffs.py). They are exploratory evidence, not certificates.  Not found in the literature: an explicit 'integral of negative part = O(Y^eps) iff RH' theorem. The arXiv abstract searches came back empty. It is an immediate corollary of Landau plus Littlewood and should be presented as such.

### Recommended actions

- Cite Landau's theorem in its Laplace/Mellin form for M3 and CONSUMER.MELLIN.SPECIALIZED_LANDAU: Widder 1941 Ch. II Thm 5b, Montgomery-Vaughan MNT I Lemma 15.1, Ingham 1932 Thm H. Describe M4/M7 (subpower negative mass) as a routine corollary obtained by splitting f = f_+ - f_-, not as new analysis.
- Add an R17-type converse for the fixed rows. Under RH, sum_{n<=t}(p_j*mu)(n)n^{-1/2} << t^eps, so f_j(X) = C_j log X + int_1^X(...)dt/t << X^eps, and the same holds for W_X. After review, relabel the negative-mass branches of OPEN.ARITH.ROWS23_NATIVE, OPEN.ARITH.FIVE_THREE_NEGATIVE_MASS and OPEN.ARITH.FIXED_DETECTOR_NEGATIVE_MASS from OPEN_SUFFICIENT_FOR_RH to OPEN_RH_EQUIVALENT. Keep the eventual-sign branches separate: they are RH-equivalent only modulo simple zeros plus H3' = sum 1/(gamma^2\|zeta'(rho)\|) < inf, via the Han 2026 method.
- Cite Suzuki arXiv:2411.07436 (Thm 1) and Han arXiv:2608.27130 (Thms 1.1-1.2) as the closest literature for the fixed-row detector pattern in canonical/consumers/mellin-landau/README.md. Cite Johnston (Canad. Math. Bull. 2023) and Pólya/Turán/Haselgrove for history. Record the drift constants kappa_j = C_j+P_j(1/2)/zeta(1/2), recomputed with directed arithmetic (analyst floats: 1.11171, 0.40646, 6.77792).
- Clarify what 'Open: m=1' in CURRENT_RESULTS section 2 means. If it includes pointwise positivity of H^{[1]} (kernel T=4sqrt y-3), add a firewall. By Ingham 1942, eventual one-signedness of this Pólya-class detector (bias 1.80332, coefficients ~ (1-67^{-rho})/(i gamma zeta'(rho))) would force linear relations among zeta ordinates. The RH-equivalent target is the negative-mass/variation node OPEN.ARITH.CV. Optionally, a Haselgrove/Hurst-style lattice computation could refute pointwise H^{[1]}>=0 unconditionally.
- Add a graded consumer node: theta-negative-mass is equivalent to zeta != 0 on Re s > 1/2+theta for the 1/s^2-smoothed rows (forward by Landau, converse by Titchmarsh 14.2 growth plus Mellin inversion). State in OPEN_CUTS section 1 that any unconditional partial bound with theta<1/2 in any coordinate (Q4, HCNC, XD, wavelet) would be a zero-free strip, i.e. quasi-RH.
- In the OPEN_CUTS fixed-detector firewall ('counting negative intervals does not supply the bound'), cite Kaczorowski-Pintz (Acta Math. Hungar. 1986/87) and Kaczorowski (Acta Arith. 1984/85): for no-main-term detectors such as the minimal wavelet, sign changes are >> log Y. Note that A-M03's 'arbitrarily late signs of both kinds' is the qualitative form of this.
- Cite Titchmarsh section 14.2 for the R15 part 1 reciprocal-zeta growth bound, and classical Littlewood/Titchmarsh section 14.25 for the spectral abscissa. Cite Nyman-Beurling and Báez-Duarte (arXiv:math/0202141) as the nearest relatives of the R4 suffix-field criterion. Cite Balazard-Saias-Yor (Adv. Math. 1999; see arXiv:1306.0856) for the Cauchy weight \|ds\|/\|s\|^2 structure. State explicitly that neither is equivalent at the repo's normalization.
- For Q4: keep the existing Montgomery-Vaughan 1974 citation and note that MV gives the stronger per-coefficient error T+O(n). Cite Franel-Landau (1924) and Gál / Aistleitner-Berkes-Seip (arXiv:1210.0741) for the periodized GCD-square Gram, and test whether Gál's (log log N)^2 bound sharpens the periodized squared-log estimate. That scope is periodized only.
- For the half-divisor packet, identify eta with d_{1/2} (the Dirichlet coefficients of zeta^{1/2}; Selberg-Delange, Tenenbaum Ch. II.5). Cite Hardy-Littlewood-Pólya (Inequalities, Thms 327-330) for the exact weighted Hardy constant 3. For the Vaughan rewrites, cite Vaughan 1977 and Heath-Brown 1982.
- Question for maintainers (no defect witness): claims/lemmas/L-96000 boxes '(L-96000.3) f_j(X) >= 0' as given by 'the prime-sieved theorem L-94200--L-94201'. Together with L-96000/L-96001 and T-96000, this reads as an unconditional RH route, while the integrated registry treats row positivity as open (OPEN.ARITH.ROWS23_NATIVE). Consider adding a visible scope banner to L-96000 pointing to the integrated status. Unconditional global positivity of c_X(j) would itself be RH-strength (a Suzuki/Han-type sign criterion), and no unconditional proof of any such criterion exists in the literature.
- If finite negative-mass scans are kept as evidence, attach an explicit Landau-Pintz statement (Johnston-Trudgian arXiv:2511.09978 Thm 1) quantifying what they imply. Record the authors' caveat that for zeta this cannot exceed existing RH verification, so finite scans stay exploratory.

### Independent verifier

The draft is mostly sound. Every bibliographic citation I could check exists with the stated venue, volume and pages: Landau 1905, Littlewood 1912, Pólya 1919/1930, Turán 1948, Ingham 1942, Haselgrove, Grosswald, Anderson-Stark, KP I/II, Kaczorowski I/II, Vaughan, Heath-Brown, Burnol, Báez-Duarte (Lincei), BSY, Johnston (CMB), MT (IJNT 2021), Pintz (Math. Pannonica), Johnston-Trudgian, Suzuki and Han. The full-text claims about Suzuki, Han, Báez-Duarte, ABS, BLM and JT are accurate. I independently confirmed the analyst constants kappa_2=1.11171, kappa_3=0.40646, kappa_W=6.77792, the H^{[1]} bias 1.80332 and the G_1 bias 2.40443, plus the identity 5P_2+3P_3=-3(a-1)(a-2). The R17/Littlewood converse extends to rows 2, 3 and W_X as claimed.  Main defects:  (1) PA2 calls M4/M7 a generalization. In fact they are the classical one-sided (holomorphic-defect) Landau theorem with B=f_-, i.e. a special case.  (2) PA7 and PA8 assert that the repo criteria are 'not equivalent' to Nyman-Beurling or BSY. Every one of these criteria is RH-equivalent, so they are logically equivalent to each other. Only a direct structural translation is missing; the recommended action repeats the error.  (3) OB4 asserts Theta(log Y) sign-change counts and an O(log Y) cap under RH. The literature has lower bounds only, and the upper-bound claim is false in general.  (4) OB5 transfers the Nyman-Beurling log-defect to the repo's suffix-field space without any theorem. It also misattributes the bound to Balazard-Saias; the source is Báez-Duarte-Balazard-Landreau-Saias 2000.  (5) Smaller citation and scope slips: - PA4: L_alpha is treated in MT 2012/2017, not in IJNT 2021. - PA13: the HLP theorem is probably 256, not 257. - OB2: Humphries also assumes a negative-moment bound. - OB7: Thm 7 averages over moduli q<=Q with Q>=x, and the individual counterpart is the one-directional Thm 6(1). - OB3: 'every fixed detector in the class' overreaches. - PA5: ROWS23 has a holomorphic-defect branch, not a literal negative-mass branch. The disjunctive nodes are RH-equivalent as whole statements.  (6) Outside the audited items, TT6's '(1/3) times the Franel kernel' is wrong. Exactly, (g^2-1)/(4de)=3*Franel(d,e)-1/(4de), and (g^2-1)/12 is the complete-residue covariance of x mod d and x mod e. I verified this in exact rational arithmetic. The PA9 constant comparison can now be stated: MV's uniform form gives 2T+2piX<2T+14X.  Evidence levels: the drafted memory_unverified items PA1, PA6, PA9, PA10 and PA13 can be upgraded to secondary_source or fulltext_read on the basis of the checks above.

**Missed prior art flagged:**
- E. Alkan, 'Variations on criteria of Pólya and Turán for the Riemann hypothesis', J. Number Theory 225 (2021) 90-124 (zbMATH 1500.11064). Sign conditions on L_alpha and its alpha-derivatives (weighted Liouville sums, log n weights) are equivalent to RH for 1/2<alpha<1. This is the direct precedent for fixed weighted sign detectors and for Han's log n weighting.
- N. Ng, 'The distribution of the summatory function of the Möbius function', Proc. LMS (3) 89 (2004) 361-389 (zbMATH 1138.11341). Under RH, LI and J_{-1}(T)<<T, it gives a limiting log-distribution for M(x)/sqrt x. This is the right tool, besides ANS/Humphries, for classifying Pólya-class and no-main-term detectors (wavelet/XD) and for H3'-type hypotheses.
- M. Milinovich, N. Ng, 'Lower bounds for moments of zeta'(rho)', arXiv:0706.2321 (IMRN 2014), and S. M. Gonek, Invent. Math. 75 (1984). These are the actual inputs behind the OB1 divergence argument and behind the plausibility of Han-type H3' conditions for the row converse (TT1).
- S. Bettin, J. B. Conrey, D. W. Farmer, 'An optimal choice of Dirichlet polynomials for the Nyman-Beurling criterion', arXiv:1211.5191 (Sovrem. Probl. Mat. 16, Steklov 2012, 38-44). Under RH plus a J_{-1} hypothesis, the Möbius-weighted coefficients mu(n)(1-log n/log N) attain the optimal ~C/log N distance. This is closest to the R4 suffix-field and the Selberg-mollified wavelet energy.
- Báez-Duarte, Balazard, Landreau, Saias, 'Notes sur la fonction zeta de Riemann, 3', Adv. Math. 149 (2000) 130-144. This is the actual source of the C/sqrt(log N) lower bound attributed in OB5.
- K. Conrad, 'Partial Euler products on the critical line', Canad. J. Math. 57 (2005), and H. Akatsuka (cited as [2] in Suzuki 2411.07436). These are critical-normalization asymptotics with log drift, stronger-than-RH counterparts that Suzuki contrasts with his Thm 1. They are relevant to the drift class (rows 2, 3, W_X).
- M. J. Mossinghoff, T. S. Trudgian, 'The Liouville function and the Riemann hypothesis', in Exploring the Riemann Zeta Function (Springer 2017) 201-221, and MT 2012 (J. Aust. Math. Soc. 93). Ingham's Fejér-kernel method extended to the weighted family L_alpha. This is the direct template for applying Ingham to H^{[1]} (OB1, TT3).
- V. I. Vasyunin, 'On a biorthogonal system related with the Riemann hypothesis', St. Petersburg Math. J. 7 (1996) 405-419. Explicit Gram entries of dilated fractional-part functions (cotangent/GCD-type sums), relevant to the Q4 Jordan GCD-square Gram and the wavelet Gram structure.
- D. J. Platt, T. S. Trudgian, 'The Riemann hypothesis is true up to 3·10^12', Bull. LMS (2021). This is the calibration fact used by OB6/TT5 and should be cited directly.
- A. Dixit, S. Chorge, 'Voronoi summation formulas, oscillations of Riesz sums, and Ramanujan-Guinand and Cohen-type identities', Proc. Roy. Soc. Edinburgh A (2026). Covers oscillation of Riesz sums of lambda and mu (cited in Han's introduction) and is relevant to the hinge/Riesz-mean detectors (PA15).

### Known obstructions

- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O1** Pointwise-envelope certificates of window Weil positivity need frequency resolution T1(L)=2πe^{A_L} with A_L ~ 4e^L, i.e. doubly exponential in L. No pointwise bound on the prime comb lowers this, because sup of the comb equals A_L. — *Zhu, arXiv:2608.24827v2, Theorem 1.4 and Lemma 3.2 (unconditional)*. How it constrains: The repo's O2 coercivity uses exactly the crude comb bound (cos ≤ 1, i.e. Q_X), so its Schur block grows doubly exponentially in L. From the O2 conditions (S_m ≥ 7+2Q_X, π^2(K+1)^2/L^2 ≥ 2C_m+7/2) I computed the minimal K: about 96 at L=1, 1.2e3 at L=1.5, 1.2e4 at L=2, 1.4e7 at L=3, 1.6e12 at L=4 (my arithmetic from the repo's displayed inequalities; the repo uses K=101 at L=1). An 'unbounded prescribed family of full windows' cannot be reached by this envelope. Any all-L argument needs signed cancellation in the prime comb. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O2** The positive margin of window Weil positivity collapses super-exponentially. ε_λ ≤ Cδ_4(λ)(log λ)^3 with δ_n(λ) ~ c_nλ^{2n+1}e^{-4πλ^2} is stated as a theorem (Connes–Kastoris), log-window half-width log λ. Zhu proves λ*(L) ≤ exp(-Le^L) under RH and measures 10^-283 at L=2. — *Andrews–Connes–La Gatta arXiv:2611.13129, eq. (4) and Thm 3.2 (citing Kastoris, zenodo 10.5281/zenodo.22864295); Zhu arXiv:2608.24827 conditional theorem and Table 3*. How it constrains: A lower certificate for a large window must resolve astronomically small margins unless the target is relaxed (see tool T2: ε_L ≥ -η(L), η→0). Numerical positivity at moderate L says little about uniformity. Float64 evaluation of the geometric side produces spurious negative eigenvalues (Zhu §10; Groskin's inconclusive band). [secondary_source; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O3** Finite Galerkin truncations certify only within a fixed band. An eigenvalue in [-B_T,0) of the archimedean-cutoff matrix certifies nothing, and band-N positivity is not continuum positivity. — *Groskin, arXiv:2607.02828v3, Cor. 3.3*. How it constrains: This confirms the repo's rule that positive Galerkin S_m are upper approximations. Band-limited certificates from the CvS/CCM literature cannot be imported as the repo's continuum lower certificate. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O4** Newman's conjecture holds: Λ ≥ 0. For every t<0 the heat-flow deformation H_t of Ξ has non-real zeros. — *B. Rodgers, T. Tao, 'The de Bruijn–Newman constant is non-negative', Forum Math. Pi 8 (2020) e6, arXiv:1801.05914; Polymath15, Res. Math. Sci. 6 (2019) 31 (Λ ≤ 0.22)*. How it constrains: The repo's abstract First-Hermite theorem (ZH.17) applies to the zero multiset of H_t, which is conjugation-invariant, in a strip and of subquadratic count (my inference). So for every t<0, M_{Z_t}(q,x)<0 for some (q,x). A proof of the First-Hermite inequality, or of any heat-type positivity, must use an input destroyed by arbitrarily small backward de Bruijn–Newman deformation, such as the exact Euler product or Λ(n). Functional equation, Gaussian decay, zero-counting asymptotics and 'gamma reserve versus envelope' arguments cannot suffice. RH is 'barely true'. [abstract_only; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O5** Quasi-RH barrier at the constant-four line. The repo's own source says the constant 4 is 1/(max off-line depth)^2=1/(1/2)^2. By the terminal-pair asymptotic (ZH.3), an off-line zero at depth y0 makes M negative at q ≈ loglog\|x\|/y0^2. Uniform positivity for q ≤ (4+δ)loglog\|x\| would therefore (heuristically) exclude zeros with β>1/2+1/√(4+δ), a zero-free strip Re s>1-η(δ). — *PR #385 GROWING_RESOLUTION_FIRST_HERMITE_WEDGE.md §1 (DW.4) and §8; zero-free regions known only of Vinogradov–Korobov width (Ford, Proc. LMS 85 (2002))*. How it constrains: Crossing the constant-four frontier uniformly in x is plausibly at least as hard as the open quasi-Riemann hypothesis. Zero-density estimates near σ=1 cannot give it pointwise. This sharpens R10: the thin boundary is not only insufficient, it is already beyond current technology. This is a heuristic: a quantitative terminal-pair lemma is needed to make it rigorous. [memory_unverified; verifier: relation_overstated]
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O6** De Branges' positivity conditions, which would imply GRH, fail for the de Branges spaces of ζ and L(s,χ4). Lax–Phillips (Appendix 2 to §7 of 'Scattering theory for automorphic functions', 1976) explained difficulties of scattering approaches. — *J. B. Conrey, X.-J. Li, 'A note on some positivity conditions related to zeta and L-functions', IMRN 2000 (arXiv:math/9812166), §1–2*. How it constrains: Hardy-space/canonical-system positivity statements stronger than RH can simply be false. For the Suzuki/Hardy route, any intermediate domination stronger than the screw/Weil sign (e.g. a specific Douglas/Stinespring contraction with a fixed delay structure) must be checked to be RH-equivalent and not merely sufficient, or it may fail. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O7** Euler-product-like systems with positive prime weights and good PNT behaviour need not satisfy RH. Beurling generalized-prime systems have zeta zeros on σ=1-1/log t (Diamond–Montgomery–Vorhauer; Zhang). Functions with the zeta-type functional equation but no Euler product have off-line zeros (Davenport–Heilbronn). — *H. G. Diamond, H. L. Montgomery, U. M. A. Vorhauer, Math. Ann. 334 (2006) 1–36; W.-B. Zhang, Math. Ann. 337 (2007) 671–704 (Zbl 1121.11061); H. Davenport, H. Heilbronn, J. London Math. Soc. 11 (1936)*. How it constrains: Mechanisms whose only arithmetic input is positivity of prime/Jordan weights (positive Poisson colligations, positive c_a(n), positive source atoms) cannot prove the RH-equivalent sign. They need gamma-factor and exact-coefficient fidelity. This supports the repo's REFUTATIONS §1 'keep a literal source identity'. [secondary_source; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O8** Fixed-degree or fixed-order positivity driven by a universal Hermite/Gaussian limit is RH-blind. — *Griffin–Ono–Rolen–Zagier PNAS 2019 (arXiv:1902.07321); Farmer arXiv:2008.07206*. How it constrains: This supports REFUTATION.FREDHOLM.FIXED_DEGREE_HIGH_CARRIER. It warns that Q4.HEAT.PHASE_LOCKED_SUBLINEAR (fixed/sublinear order) cannot be promoted to all-order positivity by asymptotics alone. [abstract_only; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O9** Continuity of the window ground-state energy in the window length is analytically delicate. Bombieri's claimed continuity ([2000, Thm 5]) 'calls for a more careful analysis'; Suzuki proves continuity of λ_a by the screw-function expansion. — *M. Suzuki, arXiv:2606.09096 §1.1–1.2, Theorem 1.3*. How it constrains: Any repo argument that propagates a sign across window lengths (e.g. from L=1 to nearby L, or a 'first degeneracy' argument) must supply continuity for its own Lorentz-weighted kernel and not import Bombieri's statement. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]

### Transplantable tools

- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-T1** Zhu's 'one-stroke' certified reduction: the frequency split Q ≥ R(f) above T♯ is an exact direct sum, with every discarded term signed. R is represented in a Legendre basis; the leading block is checked by verified Cholesky; quadrature uses Bernstein-ellipse bounds; tail deviation ε_D and coupling ε_B are explicit. (*Zhu, arXiv:2608.24827v2, Thm 1.1, §5 (certificate), §6 (odd sector)*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS §3 and the 'whole-window sign certificate' bounded contribution: a rigorous continuum lower certificate for the repo's S_L at L=1, X=3, K=101.. Next step: Rewrite the repo's form as q(h,h)=(b/2π)∫V_X(w)\|ĥ\|^2/(b^2+w^2)dw plus the exact pole/tail terms of O1/O2, without constraints. Apply Zhu's split at a T♯ where 1-C/(T♯^2+1/4) times the Lorentz weight leaves a positive floor. Represent the low-frequency block in a Legendre basis on [0,1] and certify λ_min by interval Cholesky with explicit ε_D, ε_B. This bypasses the 104-dimensional Schur complement entirely and gives the one-window sign directly, which the energy-Schur theorem then transfers to S ⪰ 0.. Risk: The Lorentz weight b/(b^2+w^2) shrinks the high-frequency floor like 1/w^2, so the envelope (1-C_m/(w^2+1/4)) is only positive beyond w ≈ √C_m ≈ 216 at L=1, and the margin there is tiny. T♯ and matrix size may be much larger than in Zhu's unweighted case. The kernel identification with the global Weil form (flagged by the repo as a separate predecessor input) must be fixed first.
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-T2** Monotone-window slack: ε_L=inf q/\|\|h\|\|^2 is nonincreasing in L for a translation-invariant kernel restricted to nested windows, so ε_L ≥ -η(L) with η→0 along a cofinal sequence implies ε_L ≥ 0 for all L. (*Andrews–Connes–La Gatta, arXiv:2611.13129, Proposition 8.17 and Lemma 8.16*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS §3 'control an unbounded prescribed family of full windows'. Next step: Verify that the repo's h-coordinate form q(h,k)=b∫∫ h̄(t)W(t-u)k(u) restricts exactly under zero extension from [0,L] to [0,L'] and is translation-invariant. The kernel W is L-independent once X ≥ e^{L'} (O1.2). Then restate the gate as an RH-equivalent target that needs no resolution of the super-exponentially small positive margin: lim inf_{L→∞} min(0, λ_min(S_L)) = 0 along any cofinal L-sequence.. Risk: The reformulation is RH-equivalent, so it moves no difficulty. It only removes the need to certify a positive margin. Monotonicity concerns the normalized Rayleigh quotient in h-coordinates; the damping f=e^{at}h preserves signs but not eigenvalues (as the repo notes).
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-T3** Prolate/Sonin conditioning: remove near-self-dual prolate modes and the pole directions to get a coercive subspace with polynomial codimension O(σ^4λ^2) in the archimedean case, instead of a pointwise envelope. (*Andrews–Connes–La Gatta arXiv:2611.13129 Theorem 8.14, Remark 8.15(b); Connes–Consani arXiv:2006.13771 Thm 1 (Sonin trace); Connes–Moscovici PNAS 2022 (arXiv:2112.05500)*). Target gap: Doubly exponential codimension growth of the repo's O2 coercive subspace (KO1).. Next step: Replace the K Dirichlet sine constraints in O2 by orthogonality to the scale-adapted prolate vectors of the window. Prove the analogue of Thm 8.14 for the Lorentz-weighted kernel in the archimedean+finite-prime case at fixed L. Compare the resulting Schur block dimension with the repo's K(L) table.. Risk: ACL's semilocal positivity needs hypothesis (H2′), which is open beyond the archimedean place. Prime terms re-introduce the comb-alignment problem; the gain may be confined to the archimedean sector.
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-T4** Exact zero-side dictionary for a truncated/windowed form: every quadratic value is an exact zero sum of an explicit band-limited test function. (*Groskin arXiv:2607.02828 Thm 2.5; Suzuki arXiv:2606.09096 Thm 1.1 (A_a as Friedrichs extension of D*G_aD)*). Target gap: The repo flags 'the terminal identification of this explicit kernel with the earlier global xi/Weil construction' as an unreviewed predecessor input (OPERATOR_AUDIT Disposition).. Next step: Derive, for constrained h, q(h,h)=Σ_ρ k_b(γ_ρ)\|ĥ(γ_ρ)\|^2 plus explicit trivial-zero and pole terms, with k_b(w)=b/(b^2+w^2) or the exact multiplier. This makes RH ⇒ q ≥ 0 on V transparent and checks the normalization constants C_b, λ_j=2j+1/2, c=1/2.. Risk: For off-line zeros the multiplier is complex, so the converse (q ≥ 0 for all L ⇒ RH) needs a separate Weil-type argument. Low risk otherwise.
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-T5** Verified eigenvalue lower bounds from residuals (Temple–Kato inequality, Lehmann–Goerisch method, Plum's homotopy method), with intervalized Gram/residual matrices. (*M. T. Nakao, M. Plum, Y. Watanabe, Numerical Verification Methods and Computer-Assisted Proofs for PDEs (Springer 2019), Ch. 10–11; F. Goerisch, H. Haunhorst, ZAMM 65 (1985)*). Target gap: Turn the repo's two-sided enclosure U-(b^2/κ)R ⪯ S ⪯ U (O4.2) into an outward-rounded certificate of λ_min(S)>0.. Next step: Choose trial corrections v_i from a large Galerkin solve. Compute U and R=⟨ΠF_{z_i},ΠF_{z_j}⟩ entrywise with interval quadrature on the exact W^{1,1} kernel, including prime-power knots and the gamma series tail. Bound operator-norm errors η_U, η_R, then run interval LDL^T on U-(15/8)R-(η_U+(15/8)η_R)I.. Risk: The residual factor b^2/κ=15/8 is fixed, so R must be very small relative to U's smallest eigenvalue. If λ_min(S) at L=1 is ~1e-6 or smaller (cf. Zhu's λ*(0.5)≈1e-6 for the unweighted form), the trial corrections need very high accuracy.
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-T6** Montgomery–Vaughan mean value theorem ∫_0^T\|Σa_n n^{-it}\|^2dt=Σ\|a_n\|^2(T+O(n)). (*H. L. Montgomery, R. C. Vaughan, 'Hilbert's inequality', J. London Math. Soc. (2) 8 (1974) 73–82*). Target gap: First-Hermite: show the constant-four wall is a worst-case (phase-alignment) wall, not an average one. This is a bounded contribution, not the criterion.. Next step: For the PR #379 prime coefficient a_n=Λ(n)n^{-1/2}(1-(log n)^2/2q)e^{-(log n)^2/4q}, Σ\|a_n\|^2 ≈ q (my computation). The mean square over x in [X,2X] is about q(2√π q^{3/2})^{-2} when e^{q/2} ≪ X, against a gamma reserve of about log X/(4√π q^{3/2}). So M(q,x)>0 outside a set of relative measure O(q/(log X)^2) for any fixed profile q ≤ (log X)^{2-ε}, far beyond 4loglog. State and prove this 'almost-all-centres' theorem with explicit constants.. Risk: It cannot close the pointwise criterion. Off-line zeros produce negativity only on x-sets of measure about q^{-1/2}, which an exceptional set can absorb. Uniformity over q needs a net and derivative bounds. The constants are my sketch and are unverified.
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-T7** Certificate principle (x,b) → (2-C(f)-b)/(1-x), shifted-block pinching, and the unconditional Montgomery theorem. (*Knausgård arXiv:2610.08965 Thm 5.1, §2; Knausgård arXiv:2609.33043 §3 (pinching credited to ainta); Baluyot–Goldston–Suriajaya–Turnage-Butterbaugh Acta Arith. 214 (2024); Alpöge–Furman arXiv:2608.13637*). Target gap: CURRENT_RESULTS §10: the 269/280-block deductions need separate trace-normalization, bounded-separation, mean-square and tail inputs before the seven-point inequality yields a proportion.. Next step: Map the repo's (S1) constants (1/3000 and 19/5000) through 269-offset averaging to the pair (x,b)=(4997/1345000, 2680/1345000), which Knausgård attributes to ainta. Then cite Thm 5.1 and its inputs as the written deduction, or reprove it in the repo's normalization.. Risk: Low mathematical risk, but the outcome (0.67300) is already superseded by 0.67353820 and other unrefereed values. The value of the work is provenance and verification, not a record.
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-T8** Kronecker/Weyl simultaneous approximation, and Bagchi's characterization of RH as strong recurrence (self-approximation) of ζ. (*Zhu arXiv:2608.24827 Lemma 3.2; B. Bagchi, Math. Z. 181 (1982) 319–334 (strong recurrence)*). Target gap: METHODOLOGY.BROWNIAN.NEW_PRODUCER: state precisely which producers are Bohr-unstable.. Next step: Formalize the repo's Bohr obstruction as a lemma. Any functional depending continuously on the finitely many phases (p^{-it})_{p≤P}, and positive for zeta at all t, must be positive at every point of the torus T^{π(P)}. Then test the proposed height-dependent producer against this lemma before building it.. Risk: Height-dependent producers with P=P(t) growing escape the fixed-P torus argument. The relevant constraint then becomes large values of short Dirichlet polynomials (resonance method), which is harder to formalize.
- **BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-T9** Beurling generalized-prime and Davenport–Heilbronn test cases as source-fidelity firewalls. (*Diamond–Montgomery–Vorhauer Math. Ann. 334 (2006); Zhang Math. Ann. 337 (2007); Davenport–Heilbronn 1936*). Target gap: OPEN.OPERATOR.SUZUKI_FIRST_CHAOS_DOMINATION and API.SUZUKI.FOCK_FIRST_CHAOS (positive Poisson colligations, positive Jordan coefficients).. Next step: Check which steps of the T-91008 domination argument use only positivity of ω_{a,c} (T-91008.8) and Euler-product shape. If a step would apply verbatim to a Beurling system with zeros near σ=1, mark that step as needing gamma-factor or exact-Λ input.. Risk: Beurling zetas lack a functional equation and gamma factor, so the analogy may break at the completed-ξ level. That would itself identify which ingredient is load-bearing.

### Novelty assessment

Classical or already in the literature: (a) The energy-Schur architecture. Finite-codimension positivity of localized Weil forms is Yoshida 1992 (Lemma 3). A discrete, bounded-below window spectrum with a ground state is in Connes–Consani–Moscovici (Thm 3.6). Schur-complement reduction of window Weil positivity to a finite block, with window monotonicity and an 'η→0 suffices' corollary, is Andrews–Connes–La Gatta (Nov 2026, Lemma 8.16 and Props 8.17–8.18). Negative-index equality is Haynsworth-type inertia additivity. Galerkin upper monotonicity is Rayleigh–Ritz. (b) Cardinal capture is a careful, multiplicity-aware re-proof of the converse direction of Weil's 1952 criterion. (c) L-91035 is identical to Suzuki 2012 (Prop 2.1, Thm 2.2, Lemma 4.1) plus Lagarias 2005. T-91008's equivalence rests on Suzuki 2023 Thm 1.2. (d) The Brownian Bohr instability is the Kronecker/Bohr phenomenon, stated precisely by Zhu 2026 (Lemma 3.2). Fixed-degree high-carrier blindness parallels Griffin–Ono–Rolen–Zagier/Farmer. (e) The conjunctive APIs are classical (M-matrix Neumann series, Schur test, Poincaré). (f) The seven-point inequality and the 0.6730085 scalar come from the external ainta repository (the repo's declared source). They belong to a dense 2026 cluster (Alpöge–Furman, Lamzouri, Chandru, trmdy, Knausgård) with a written certificate principle. The scalar is identical to ainta's constant as quoted by Knausgård and is superseded by 0.67353820 (unrefereed, Lean-checked modulo search axioms).  Possibly new in exact form, though modest: - The specific Lorentz-weighted, damped window kernel with an exact complete-tail formula (O1.2). - An unconditional explicit-rational coercivity on a 104-codimensional subspace at L=1, using only a primitive-norm (H^{-1}-type) bound rather than an L2 gap. - An energy-completion Schur theorem that tolerates Riesz vectors outside L2, with the two-sided residual enclosure U-3R ⪯ S ⪯ U (U-(15/8)R at L=1). The Andrews–Connes–La Gatta lemma assumes an L2 gap, so the repo's form-norm version is a genuine but small generalization. No rigorous sign for S is claimed by either.  First-Hermite: a search of the arXiv API, zbMATH and the web found no prior statement of the zero-heat criterion RH iff Σ(γ-x)^2e^{-q(γ-x)^2} ≥ 0. Nor did it find the threat-graph terminal-pair argument or the countable reduction. The closest analogues are Weil's criterion (of which it is a restricted-family strengthening) and Bombieri–Lagarias' abstract multiset Li criterion. The unconditional (4-ε)loglog wedge uses a standard mechanism: the archimedean term beats the trivially bounded prime sum. The constant-four frontier coincides with the trivial off-line depth 1/2, so crossing it uniformly is heuristically quasi-RH-hard.  Uncertainty: Yoshida and Bombieri were assessed only via zbMATH reviews and Suzuki's 2026 survey. Several 2026 comparators (Zhu, Groskin, Andrews–Connes–La Gatta, Knausgård, Suzuki 2026) are unrefereed preprints. Zhu itself retracted an earlier support-2.38 claim. Web-search sources consulted: [Zhu 2608.24827](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.24827), [Li's criterion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li%27s_criterion), [arXiv 2311.08519](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.08519). Full texts were read via arXiv for 2608.24827, 2611.13129, 2606.09096, 2607.02828, 2006.13771, 1204.1827, math/9812166, 2610.08965, 2609.33043 and 2609.02882.

### Recommended actions

- In heat_hermite/FIRST_HERMITE_REGIONS.md and CURRENT_RESULTS §7, cite Weil (1952) and Bombieri–Lagarias (JNT 77, 1999, abstract multiset Li criterion) as the nearest prior art for HEAT.HERMITE.COUNTABLE_CRITERION. Record that no earlier statement of the zero-heat/First-Hermite criterion was located (searched arXiv, zbMATH, web).

Suzuki, arXiv:1204.1827v2, Prop 2.1, Thm 2.2, Lemma 4.1, together with Lagarias, Acta Arith. 120 (2005), for unconditional innerness of Θ_ω when ω ≥ 1/2. For T-91008, pin Suzuki, J. London Math. Soc. 108 (2023) Thm 1.2 (the screw-function criterion).
- Add Andrews–Connes–La Gatta (arXiv:2611.13129, Lemma 8.16, Props 8.17–8.18, Thm 8.14) and Yoshida 1992 (Lemmas 2–3, Prop 1) as prior/parallel work for the energy-Schur reduction in CURRENT_RESULTS §7. State the delta: the repo needs only form-norm coercivity, and its Riesz vectors may lie outside L2.
- Record in OPEN_CUTS §3 the known barrier for unbounded window families. Zhu (arXiv:2608.24827) Thm 1.4/Lemma 3.2 show pointwise-envelope certificates need doubly exponential resolution. The repo's own O2 constants give coercive codimension K ≈ 96, 1.2e4, 1.4e7 and 1.6e12 at L = 1, 2, 3, 4. So the 'unbounded family of full windows' requires signed prime-comb cancellation, not a better envelope.
- Restate the energy-Schur all-L gate in the slack form of ACL Prop 8.17: min(0, λ_min(S_L)) → 0 along a cofinal sequence. This is still RH-equivalent but avoids certifying super-exponentially small positive margins. First verify window-monotonicity of the repo's h-coordinate Rayleigh quotient.
- For the bounded contribution 'whole-window sign certificate', adapt Zhu's one-stroke reduction (verified Cholesky + Bernstein-ellipse quadrature + explicit tail-coupling bound) to the repo's Lorentz-weighted kernel at L=1. Alternatively, intervalize the repo's own U-(15/8)R enclosure with Temple/Lehmann–Goerisch-style residual control. Either output is a certified λ_min(S_1)>0 or a precise obstruction.
- Before relying on the explicit-kernel identification (flagged in OPERATOR_AUDIT 'Disposition' as an unreviewed predecessor input), derive the zero-side dictionary for the constrained Lorentz-weighted form, in the style of Groskin Thm 2.5. Check the constants C_b, λ_j=2j+1/2, c=1/2 and the damping a=3/4.
- Question for the First-Hermite programme: can the terminal-pair asymptotic (ZH.3) be made quantitative? The target is a lemma that an off-line zero at depth y0 and height t0 forces M(q,x)<0 for some q ≤ (1+o(1))loglog t0/y0^2. With it, uniform positivity slightly above constant four would imply a zero-free strip Re s>1-η, i.e. the open quasi-Riemann hypothesis, and R10's boundary could be labelled accordingly.
- Optional bounded contribution: prove an 'almost-all-centres' First-Hermite positivity theorem beyond 4loglog using the Montgomery–Vaughan mean value theorem. Label it explicitly as non-pointwise; it cannot replace the R10 complement.
- Add the de Bruijn–Newman constraint (Rodgers–Tao, Forum Math. Pi 2020) to REFUTATIONS §2. The abstract First-Hermite theorem applies to the zeros of H_t, so any proof of First-Hermite or heat positivity must use input that fails for every t<0 (exact Euler product or Λ(n)). Gamma-reserve or envelope-only arguments are excluded.
- In CURRENT_RESULTS §10, cite the 2026 literature. Alpöge–Furman (arXiv:2608.13637) and Lamzouri (arXiv:2609.02882) prove 0.6725 unconditionally. Knausgård (arXiv:2610.08965, Thm 5.1) states the certificate principle and identifies (x,b)=(4997/1345000, 2680/1345000) as ainta's constant, which equals the repo's R ≈ 0.6730085. Knausgård proves 0.67353820 (unrefereed). State that the repo's scalar is not a record and that the 269/280 deduction is Thm 5.1 with the BGST unconditional Montgomery theorem.
- In operator_no_go, cite Griffin–Ono–Rolen–Zagier (PNAS 2019) and Farmer (arXiv:2008.07206) next to REFUTATION.FREDHOLM.FIXED_DEGREE_HIGH_CARRIER. Cite Zhu Lemma 3.2 (Kronecker/Weyl optimality of the comb constant) and Bagchi's strong-recurrence theorem next to REFUTATION.BROWNIAN.COFINAL_STABILITY.
- For OPEN.WEIL.CORRECTED_ARITHMETIC_FLOOR's 'odd half-line sign' alternative, cite Yoshida 1992 Prop. 1 (odd-sector Weil positivity implies RH) and check that the repo's odd normalization matches it.
- For the Suzuki/Hardy route, record Conrey–Li (IMRN 2000), who show that de Branges' positivity conditions fail for ζ, as a caution: every intermediate domination in T-91008 must be shown RH-equivalent, not merely sufficient. Test the first-chaos argument against a Beurling generalized-prime system (Diamond–Montgomery–Vorhauer 2006; Zhang 2007) to see which steps need gamma-factor or exact-Λ input.
- When propagating signs across window lengths, do not import Bombieri's continuity claim (2000, Thm 5). Use Suzuki arXiv:2606.09096 Thm 1.3, or prove continuity for the repo's kernel.

### Independent verifier

The draft is generally careful and most citations hold up. I checked the large majority against full texts in the cache: Andrews–Connes–La Gatta 2611.13129 §8.6, Zhu 2608.24827v2, Suzuki 2606.09096v4, 1204.1827v2 and 2206.03682, Connes–Consani–Moscovici 2511.22755, Connes–Consani 2006.13771, Groskin 2607.02828v3, Knausgård 2610.08965 and 2609.33043, Alpöge–Furman, Lamzouri, Conrey–Li, Burnol, Rodgers–Tao, Griffin–Ono–Rolen–Zagier and Farmer. Classical items were checked via zbMATH: Bombieri 2000, Yoshida 1992, Bombieri–Lagarias, Haynsworth, Goldston–Gonek, Carneiro–Chandee–Milinovich and Zhang. I independently recomputed two numerical claims, and both reproduce exactly. First, R=(1345000H0-2680)/1340003 equals Knausgård's (H0-b)/(1-x) at ainta's pair, 0.67300852792778. Second, the KO1 codimension table from the repo's O2 inequalities: K = 96, 1222, 11577, 1.37e7 and 1.58e12 at L = 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4.  Main defects: (1) KO5 rests on an outdated premise. The Observatory's own history file records OpenAI's Sep–Oct 2026 quasi-RH claims (Re s>7/8 and 11/12), so 'zero-free regions known only of VK width' and 'beyond current technology' are not supportable. Under that claim the depth heuristic would move the frontier toward 64/9, not certify a barrier. (2) PA14 misdescribes Littlewood 1924. The unconditional gap theorem is triple-log and proved by complex analysis; the loglog explicit-formula mechanism is RH-conditional. (3) PA08 'repo_generalizes' overstates. Infinite-dimensional inertia and Schur statements are standard. (4) KO1's closing sentence turns Zhu's barrier, proved only for pointwise-envelope certificates, into a claim about every all-L argument. (5) PA03's 'prediction' for neg-index(S_L) ignores the damped Lorentz kernel and the unreviewed kernel/xi identification. The better repo comparator is PR #373's quarantined negative-index claim. (6) PA09 misses the direct precedent for the capture step: Yoshida Lemma 1 and Prop 1, and Connes–Consani Appendix C. (7) PA20 should point to the specific quarantined object, T-90505 in PR #368. (8) Minor attribution issues. Closedness of QW^a belongs to Connes–Consani 2023 rather than CCM Thm 3.6. Zhang 2007, not DMV, states the σ=1-1/log t zeros. Knausgård quotes ainta's pair via Chandru. Zhu's λ*(0.5) is an upper bound. (9) Several evidence levels are understated, because full texts were cached for CCM, GORZ, Farmer and Rodgers–Tao.  The novelty assessment broadly stands with one sharpening. ACL's Lemma 8.16 already allows form-bounded coupling, so the repo's real delta is only the absence of an L2 gap, plus negative-index equality, Galerkin monotonicity and the residual enclosure. The claim that no prior First-Hermite statement exists remains a negative search result; arXiv:2607.04316 should be checked first. KO4's de Bruijn–Newman barrier is a sound heuristic, but its strip and counting hypotheses for H_t at t<0 should be stated explicitly. Missed bridge worth recording: Suzuki's Prop 1.2 ties innerness of Θ_ω to zero-free half-planes, so the claimed quasi-RH would enlarge L-91035's safe range to a>3/8.

**Missed prior art flagged:**
- Yoshida 1992 (Adv. Stud. Pure Math. 21) Lemma 1 and Prop 1, and Connes–Consani, Selecta Math. 27 (2021) arXiv:2006.13771, Appendix C Prop C.1. Both construct compactly supported tests with g̃0(ρ0)=1, small at the other zeros and vanishing on a prescribed finite set. This is the direct precedent for the R16 cardinal-capture interpolation step, and a closer one than Weil 1952.
- Suzuki arXiv:1204.1827 Prop 1.2 (via Lagarias–Suzuki, JNT 118 (2006) Thm 4). For ω0≥0, ζ(s)≠0 on Re s>1/2+ω0 if and only if Θ_ω is meromorphic inner for every ω>ω0. If the 2026 quasi-RH (Re s>7/8) is accepted, this would extend L-91035's unconditional isometry range from a>1/2 to a>3/8. That is a concrete missed bridge.
- OpenAI, 'The Quasi-Riemann Hypothesis' manuscripts: zero-free Re s>7/8 (30 Sep 2026) and Re s>11/12 (5 Oct 2026), for ζ and all Dirichlet L-functions. They are unrefereed but have a Lean statement for 7/8. They bear directly on KO5 and on any depth-based reading of the First-Hermite constant four: the heuristic depth bound 1/y0^2 becomes ≥64/9 under the 7/8 claim.
- H. L. Montgomery, 'Zeros of approximations to the zeta function' (Studies in Pure Mathematics, Birkhäuser 1983), with P. Turán (1948) on partial sums. This is the classical Kronecker/Bohr refutation of zero-free finite Dirichlet approximants, the nearest precedent for REFUTATION.BROWNIAN.COFINAL_STABILITY. It is closer than Zhu Lemma 3.2.
- A. Connes, W. D. van Suijlekom, 'Quadratic forms, real zeros and echoes of the spectral action', arXiv:2511.23257 (cached). It gives explicit Galerkin truncations of the Weil form at a prime cutoff, the direct literature comparator for the repo's O4 finite effective matrices S_m.
- A. Connes, C. Consani, 'Spectral triples and ζ-cycles', Enseign. Math. 69 (2023) 93–148. It is the source of lower-boundedness and lower semicontinuity of the localized Weil form (PA04 attribution).
- C. Tretter, Spectral Theory of Block Operator Matrices and Applications (Imperial College Press, 2008), and Pontryagin/Krein negative-index theory with Glazman's lemma. These are the standard infinite-dimensional Schur-complement and inertia tools behind O4.1 (PA08).
- J. E. Littlewood (1924) and R. R. Hall, W. K. Hayman (2000) on unconditional gaps ≪1/logloglog γ, via complex analysis. Littlewood's RH-conditional S(t) ≪ log t/loglog t gives the correct calibration for PA14: the loglog-scale explicit-formula mechanism is conditional for zero location and unconditional only for weighted all-zero sums.
- Possibly related, not verified: arXiv:2607.04316, 'A Gaussian-Perron Prime-Side Defect and Local Profiles Near Critical-Line Zeros'. It uses a Gaussian-smoothed explicit formula with an 'explicit boundary separating amplified and suppressed nonlocal zero contributions', a possible parallel to the ZH.7 threat-graph boundary. It should be read before claiming novelty of the First-Hermite terminal-pair device.

**Programme scope examined:** ROBIN (Robin arithmetic: finite barrier 5041..5582, Hardy-Ramanujan canonical reduction, nested-level encoding, powered bounded-tail envelope with R6 cap/feasibility contract; OPEN_CUTS section 2 "arithmetic-tail")
