### Known obstructions

- **BRG-ROBIN-O1** If RH is false, Robin's inequality fails for infinitely many n, by a secondary-term margin. There exist 0<beta<1/2 (any 1-b<beta<1/2, with b=Re(rho)>1/2) and C>0 such that sigma(n) >= e^g n loglog n + C n loglog n/(log n)^beta infinitely often. — *Robin 1984, Section 4, Proposition 1, as restated in Lagarias, arXiv:math/0008177, Proposition 3.2.*. How it constrains: A theorem covering the whole unbounded canonical tail is RH itself. No 'complete infinite pruning argument' weaker than RH can exist. Any proposed unconditional tail theorem must contain RH-strength analytic input, i.e. a bound on prime counts at the scale x^{1/2+o(1)}. [fulltext_read (Lagarias restatement; Robin original not accessed); verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-ROBIN-O2** Under RH the Robin margin on the extremal (SA/CA) numbers tends to zero: limsup (sigma(n)/n - e^g loglog n) sqrt(log n) <= -e^g(2sqrt2 - 4 - g + log 4pi) ~ -1.393 (Ramanujan, assuming RH). The explicit form is sigma(n)/n <= e^g(loglog n - 2(sqrt2-1)/sqrt(log n) + S_1(log n) + O(...)), where S_1 is a sum over zeta zeros. — *S. Ramanujan, 'Highly composite numbers', annotated by J.-L. Nicolas and G. Robin, Ramanujan J. 1 (1997) 119-153 (zbMATH 0917.11043), sections 59-71; restated in Nicolas-Sondow arXiv:1211.6944 section 1, CNS arXiv:1110.5078 Proposition 1, Axler arXiv:2110.13478 final remark.*. How it constrains: Finite pruning and envelopes with a fixed relative margin cannot close the tail. The margin shrinks like (log n)^{-1/2} and oscillates with the zeros through S_1. [secondary_source; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-ROBIN-O3** For every fixed n0, limsup_{a->inf} G(a n0) = e^g. Any subtree with a fixed prefix and an unrestricted continuation has no margin. A margin exists only once continuation exponents are capped, and with cap A the asymptotic margin is about a factor prod(1-p^{-A-1}), which tends to 1 as A grows. — *Caveney-Nicolas-Sondow, Ramanujan J. 29 (2012) 359-384 (arXiv:1112.6010), Fact 2 (citing CNS 2011, Lemma 10).*. How it constrains: A subtree tail lemma must be stated per (prefix, cap A). Uniformity in A is equivalent to RH, because every n is t-free for some t. [fulltext_read; verifier: relation_overstated]
- **BRG-ROBIN-O4** The t-free (Sole-Planat Psi_t) method stalls with current explicit inputs. Morrill-Platt report that both their finite-range and asymptotic bounds exceed 1 at t=21. Axler reached t=21 only with improved Mertens-product estimates. The method's margin e^g(1-1/zeta(t)) ~ e^g 2^{-t} must dominate explicit prime-counting errors. — *Morrill-Platt 2021, Section 5 'Comments'. Axler 2023, Theorem 1.2.*. How it constrains: Exponent-uniform 'tail lemmas' are capped by the precision of explicit PNT and Mertens bounds. Pushing t up by one requires better explicit inputs; no t-free argument reaches unbounded exponents. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-ROBIN-O5** Size of the canonical search domain: HR integers <= x number exp((1+o(1)) 2pi sqrt(log x/(3 loglog x))) (Hardy-Ramanujan 1917). SA numbers <= x number between (log x)^{1+5/48} for large x (Erdos-Nicolas 1975) and exp(O((loglog x)^2)) (Alaoglu-Erdos). CA numbers form a one-parameter family. — *Hardy-Ramanujan, Proc. LMS 16 (1917) 112-132 (via CLMS 2007 Proposition 1). Erdos-Nicolas, Bull. SMF 103 (1975) 65-90. Akbary-Friggstad 2009, p. 2 (quoting Alaoglu-Erdos).*. How it constrains: Not an obstruction to truth, but to the computational route. Canonical-tree traversal (the frozen 10^54/10^100 deposit) is super-polylogarithmically larger than SA or CA certification of the same range. Published CA-based certification already reaches 10^(10^13.11) (Morrill-Platt Theorem 13). [fulltext_read (CLMS, AF statements); originals secondary; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]

### Transplantable tools

- **BRG-ROBIN-T1** Robin's CA interpolation lemma combined with Briggs's CA-number algorithm, run in directed or interval arithmetic (as Morrill-Platt did) (*Robin 1984, Section 3, Proposition 1. Briggs, Exp. Math. 15 (2006). Morrill-Platt 2021, Theorem 13 (100-bit interval arithmetic).*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS arithmetic-tail 'exact finite certificate restricted to a clearly declared range', and RESULTS_INDEX 'Larger Robin certificate programme' (unreplayed 10^54/10^100 canonical traversals).. Next step: Enumerate consecutive CA pairs (N,N') up to X by ordering the critical values F(p,k)=log(1+1/(p+...+p^k))/log p. Certify G(N)<e^g at each N with the repo's existing directed atanh/harmonic/exp enclosures. Then cover every n in [N,N'] via the elementary convexity proof of Robin's Proposition 1. To avoid four-exponential ties, use rational eps=a/d slightly off each critical value; the repo's powered envelope with an empty prefix is exactly this rational-eps CA bound. That gives a second, independent backend for the [5041,5582] barrier and a route to any declared range.. Risk: The interpolation needs a complete, correctly ordered CA list, so tie handling at critical eps must be certified. The result only re-certifies ranges already published (Morrill-Platt). Its value is independent exact replay, not new mathematics.
- **BRG-ROBIN-T2** Akbary-Friggstad least-counterexample-is-SA, combined with Alaoglu-Erdos exponent relations for SA numbers (*Akbary-Friggstad 2009, Theorem 3. Alaoglu-Erdos 1944, Theorem 2 (k_r in {beta-1, beta, beta+1}, beta=floor(k_q log q/log r)) and q^{k_q} < 2^{k_2+2}, as restated in Nazardonyavi-Yakubovich arXiv:1211.2147, Proposition 4.3 and Remark 4.8. NY Theorem 4.4: floor(log p/log q) <= k_q.*). Target gap: R6 'certified caps' for bounded subtrees when the goal is 'no violation <= B'.. Next step: For range statements, restrict candidates to SA numbers (valid because the least violator <= B would be SA). Derive certified caps and floors from Alaoglu-Erdos Theorem 2 relative to the prefix exponents and the largest prime. Feed them into the R6 contract as m_i, together with Axler Theorem 1.4 floors as imported constraints.. Risk: These caps are valid only for SA candidates, not for every admissible completion. They must not be inserted into the pointwise envelope theorem, only into a separately stated range-certificate theorem. The Alaoglu-Erdos original was only seen via secondary sources.
- **BRG-ROBIN-T3** The Sole-Planat generalized Dedekind function Psi_t, with explicit Mertens-product and theta bounds, adapted to fixed-prefix, capped canonical subtrees (*Sole-Planat, Integers 12 (2012). Morrill-Platt 2021, sections 1-3. Axler 2023, Lemma 2.4 and Theorem 1.3. Dusart 2018 explicit bounds.*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS arithmetic-tail 'a lemma with explicit dependence on support, exponent ... parameters'.. Next step: Fix a prefix P=prod_{i<=m} p_i^{b_i} and a tail cap A. Bound I(h) <= I(P) prod_{m<i<=k}(1-p_i^{-A-1})/(1-p_i^{-1}) and log h >= log P + theta(p_k) - theta(p_m). Using explicit Mertens bounds, prove Robin for all k >= k0(P,A) with explicit k0, leaving a finite remainder for the envelope or a CA check. Empty P with A=t-1 recovers the t-free theorems.. Risk: The margin prod_{p not dividing P}(1-p^{-A-1}) tends to 1 as A grows (O3/O4). This yields bounded contributions only, never the tail, and current explicit inputs already stall near A=20.
- **BRG-ROBIN-T4** Support-size finiteness bound (elementary), linked to Nicolas's criterion (*Vojak arXiv:2005.09307, Theorem 1.4 and Corollary 1.5. Nicolas, J. Number Theory 17 (1983).*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS arithmetic-tail support dependence; packet section 3 'support size and exponents remain unbounded'.. Next step: State and prove natively: if omega(h)=k and h violates Robin, then log h < exp(e^-g N_k/phi(N_k)), so the class omega=k is finite. Certify the bound B_k with directed enclosures; floating-point estimates give B_10~10^15.2, B_25~10^46.2, B_30~10^57.9. Then any replayed and authenticated finite certificate to B would yield the infinite-class theorem 'Robin holds for all n>5040 with omega(n)<=k0(B)'. Note that M(k)<=0 exactly when Nicolas's inequality fails at N_k.. Risk: The resulting theorems are much weaker than what Morrill-Platt's range already implies (omega up to about 10^12). This is useful as a correctly quantified bounded contribution, not as progress on RH.
- **BRG-ROBIN-T5** Closed form for the envelope DP: separable Lagrangian (CA-type) solution (*Monotonicity of F(t,k) in both t and k: Caveney-Nicolas-Sondow arXiv:1112.6010, eqs. (7)-(9); Alaoglu-Erdos 1944. Lagrangian framing: Robin, RAIRO 1983, section 1.*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS 'Bounded contributions: independent exact finite arithmetic'; R6 envelope second backend.. Next step: Add the lemma V_2(n) = prod_{r=2}^{A} prod_{i<=n_r} max(1, g_{i,r}^d / q_i^a). Proof: w_{i,r}>1 iff F(q_i,r)>a/d. F decreases in q and r, so the positive set is a prefix L_r that is nonincreasing in r, and min(L_r,n_r) is a nested maximizer. Use this as an independent exact check of every DP value. Choose (a,d) with a/d near the CA parameter matched to the budget (eps ~ F(x,1) with x ~ log B).. Risk: Low. This is my own derivation, verified only by exact-rational comparison on 300 random instances and the R6 fixture (0 mismatches). It needs the usual review before integration.
- **BRG-ROBIN-T6** Nicolas 'benefit' method and Everett's generalized Lagrange multipliers for filling gaps between relaxation optima (*Robin, RAIRO 1983, section 3 (benefit B(n) relative to the bracketing superior HC numbers). Nicolas, Asterisque 24-25 (1975) and 38-39 (1976). Everett, Oper. Res. 11 (1963).*). Target gap: Efficient complete enumeration of near-extremal (SA) numbers between consecutive CA numbers for range certificates. AF note there is no known guaranteed fast algorithm for listing all SA numbers below a bound.. Next step: Define the benefit of n relative to the bracketing CA pair, ben(n) = eps log(n/N) - log(I(n)/I(N)) >= 0. Bound the benefit any SA number in [N,N'] can carry, and enumerate only completions with benefit below that bound, using the repo's nested-level encoding as the enumeration skeleton.. Risk: The benefit bounds for sigma (as opposed to d(n)) must be derived and certified. The method is classical for HC numbers, but I did not find a published certified implementation for SA numbers.

### Novelty assessment

Classical or known, with high confidence after full-text reading: (i) The canonical transform and the 'complete canonical reduction' (packet sections 2-3) match the Hardy-Ramanujan-integer reduction in Choie-Lichiardopol-Moree-Sole 2007, section 5 (Proposition 1, Lemmas 7-10), including the name. They also match Vojak 2020, Theorems 2.3-2.4, which even uses the same letter H. The exchange lemma goes back to Ramanujan 1915 and Alaoglu-Erdos 1944, Theorem 1. (ii) Stronger classical reductions exist: Akbary-Friggstad 2009 (the least counterexample is superabundant, using a 5041..10080 barrier) and Robin 1984, Section 3, Proposition 1 (a CA counterexample exists if any counterexample does). The repo correctly says canonical is not CA, but does not record that these stronger reductions are standard. (iii) The nested prefix-level encoding is the conjugate-partition / product-of-primorials representation (CNS 2012, eqs. 10-11). (iv) The powered envelope is a localized, capped, exact-rational form of the classical CA/Lagrangian bound sigma(n)/n^{1+eps} <= sigma(N_eps)/N_eps^{1+eps}. Its DP-with-cap-vector organization parallels Robin 1983 (RAIRO) for highly composite numbers. Its DP collapses to the classical separable closed form; that closed form is my derivation, checked exactly. (v) The finite barrier lies far inside known verified ranges: Robin 1984 numerics to 55440, Briggs 2006, and Morrill-Platt 2021 Theorem 13 to 10^(10^13.11485).  Possibly new, with moderate or low confidence: (a) a fully directed, exact-integer/dyadic self-contained certificate of the small barrier; (b) explicit identification of 5583 as the exact threshold where e^g loglog n first exceeds I(5040). This is implicit in AF-style arguments, but I did not find it stated; (c) the explicit treatment of the boundary case H(n)<=5040, which the printed proofs of CLMS Proposition 1 and Vojak Theorem 2.4 leave implicit (a rigor improvement, not a new theorem); (d) the specific fixed-prefix, capped, budgeted subtree envelope with rational eps, as a formulation.  The open cut is not new. 'Control the unbounded canonical tail' is equivalent to RH (Robin + CLMS), and the parameter-restricted versions it requests already have a published baseline: t-free for t<=21 (Axler 2023); valuation floors (Hertlein 2018, Axler 2023 Theorem 1.4); odd, squarefree and squarefull numbers (CLMS); and least-counterexample structure (Vojak 2020, Theorem 1.6).  Uncertainty and access limits: Robin 1984, Alaoglu-Erdos 1944, Briggs 2006 and Nicolas 1983 were not read in the original; their statements come from consistent secondary sources. Vojak 2020 and Wu 2019 are unrefereed preprints.  What I actually ran: - an exact-rational recomputation of both barrier maxima and their uniqueness; - double-precision (not directed) threshold values; - an exact DP-versus-closed-form comparison on 300 random instances plus the R6 fixture (0 mismatches; fixture U^{1/64}~3.8985); - floating-point support-size bounds.  No repository file was modified.

### Recommended actions

- Cite CLMS 2007 (J. Theor. Nombres Bordeaux 19; arXiv:math/0604314, section 5, Proposition 1, Lemmas 7-10) and Vojak 2020 (arXiv:2005.09307, Theorems 2.3-2.4) as prior art for packet sections 2-3 (canonical dominance and complete reduction). Cite Alaoglu-Erdos 1944 Theorem 1 and Ramanujan 1915 for the exponent-exchange principle. Note in the packet that the repo's barrier step makes the H(n)<=5040 case explicit; the printed proofs leave it implicit.
- In finite-robin-foundations.md 'Common misreadings' (or a new 'Classical context' note), record the stronger classical reductions. Akbary-Friggstad 2009 Theorem 3: the least counterexample is superabundant (SA is a subset of HR). Robin 1984 Section 3 Proposition 1: CA interpolation, so a CA counterexample exists if any does. State that the canonical reduction is a weaker, pointwise relative of these, and keep the existence and pointwise quantifiers distinct, as STRATEGIC_OUTLOOK section 5 already asks.
- State explicitly in OPEN_CUTS section 2 that 'Robin on all canonical h>5040' is equivalent to RH (Robin 1984 + CLMS Proposition 1). The unbounded tail is not a smaller problem, and a 'complete infinite pruning argument' short of RH is excluded by Robin's Omega-result (Lagarias Proposition 3.2).
- List the existing parameter-restricted theorems as the baseline any new 'tail lemma' must beat. t-free for t<=20 (Morrill-Platt Integers 21 A28) and t=21 (Axler, Ramanujan J. 61, Theorem 1.2). Valuation floors (Hertlein Integers 18 A71; Axler Theorem 1.4). Odd, squarefree and squarefull numbers (CLMS). Least-counterexample structure (Vojak Theorem 1.6). In canonical language: any canonical violator has b_1>=21 and satisfies Axler's floors up to p=1777, as imported constraints.
- Describe the [5041,5582] barrier as a self-contained directed re-certification of a small fragment of known computations (Robin 1984 to 55440; Akbary-Friggstad to 10080). Cite Morrill-Platt Theorem 13 (all 5040<n<=10^(10^13.11485), interval arithmetic) as the current published range, kept separate from the repo's accepted scope.
- Re-scope the 'larger Robin certificate programme'. For an independently replayed large range, implement CA enumeration plus Robin's interpolation lemma (elementary convexity proof) with the repo's directed enclosures, using rational eps as in the powered envelope with an empty prefix. Do not traverse the canonical tree, whose size is exp(c sqrt(log x/loglog x)) against a one-parameter CA family.
- Add the closed form V = prod_{r=2}^{A} prod_{i<=n_r} max(1, g_{i,r}^d q_i^{-a}) as a lemma and a second exact backend for the R6 envelope. Its proof uses only that F(q,r)=log g_{q,r}/log q decreases in q and r (CNS 2012 eqs. 7-9). Observe that g_{i,r}=1+1/(q_i+...+q_i^r), so the envelope is the Alaoglu-Erdos/Robin CA bound with eps=a/d. Choose a/d near the CA parameter matched to the budget.
- Add a native support-size lemma (cf. Vojak Theorem 1.4 / Corollary 1.5): a violator with omega(h)=k satisfies log h < exp(e^-g N_k/phi(N_k)), so each fixed-support canonical class is finite. This sharpens the packet's 'support size and exponents remain unbounded': exponents are bounded in terms of support for potential violators. Note the link to Nicolas 1983 (M(k)<=0 iff Nicolas fails at N_k, and then no Robin violator has omega=k).
- For bounded contributions under OPEN_CUTS section 2: (a) generalize the Sole-Planat Psi_t argument to fixed-prefix, capped subtrees to get explicit thresholds k0(P,A); (b) for range certificates only, use Akbary-Friggstad plus Alaoglu-Erdos Theorem 2 and q^{k_q}<2^{k_2+2} to certify caps for SA candidates. Keep these caps out of the pointwise envelope theorem, which needs caps valid for every admissible completion.
- Minor editorial question for reviewers: CURRENT_RESULTS section 1 uses n both for the integer and for the number of tail primes (V=V_2(n)). Consider renaming the tail length (e.g. to m) to avoid ambiguity in the reviewed statement.

### Independent verifier

The draft is unusually careful, and almost all of its full-text claims hold up. Every arXiv source it relies on was re-opened and grepped: CLMS, Vojak, Morrill-Platt, Axler, Hertlein, both CNS papers, Nazardonyavi-Yakubovich, Nicolas-Sondow, Lagarias and Wu. The Akbary-Friggstad author copy, the published Integers version of CNS 2011, and Robin's 1983 RAIRO paper were also read. All checked statements, theorem numbers and verbatim constants matched, with the exceptions below.  The core prior-art identification is correct and important. The repo's canonical transform and complete reduction (sections 2-3) are the Hardy-Ramanujan-integer reduction of CLMS 2007 section 5 and Vojak 2020 Theorems 2.3-2.4. Stronger classical existence reductions exist: Akbary-Friggstad (SA) and Robin's CA interpolation. The repository cites none of this literature; grep found no matches.  Errors and overstatements to fix: (1) Citation error: CLMS is J. Theor. Nombres Bordeaux 19(2) (2007) 357-372, not the arXiv journal-ref pages 351-366. (2) O3 misapplies CNS Fact 2. That result is about multiples a*n0. An exact-exponent canonical prefix subtree always has an unconditional asymptotic margin prod_{i<=m}(1-p_i^{-b_i-1})·prod_{p>p_m}(1-p^{-A-1}) < 1, so it holds only finitely many potential violators. The obstruction is nonuniformity, not the absence of a margin. (3) The CLMS 'implicit H(n)<=5040 case' note should acknowledge that CLMS treat this case explicitly in their Theorem 5 proof. The same boundary issue affects the CA-existence reading of Robin's Proposition 1: the bracketing pair (5040,55440) needs Robin's numerical check. (4) Morrill-Platt never mention Robin's interpolation. They attribute the method to Briggs. (5) O1's 'no pruning argument weaker than RH' is tautological. The constraint comes from Robin's equivalence itself. (6) Minor points: index shift and domain in the PA6 Lemma 7 identification; Nicolas's criterion is stated for k>=2; the NY published title differs from the arXiv title; Axler's 139<p<=1777 condition excludes v_p=1 rather than imposing a floor; Erdos-Nicolas carries an o(1) in the exponent; HR-count comparisons apply only to unpruned traversal.  Independent reproduction (my own code, exact Fractions where stated): - Barrier maxima: unique 403/105 at 5040 and 224/65 at 5460, confirmed. - Thresholds, by 50-digit mpmath intervals (not a directed certificate, since gamma entered as a point value): +0.37076 at 5041, -5.515e-6 at 5582, +3.147e-5 at 5583. - The 7920 alternative closure of the CLMS gap is valid. - DP equals the closed form on 300 random instances, with 0 mismatches. - R6 fixture: V=91.21613, U^{1/64}=3.89853, below both 39/10 and 12493/3150. - Support bounds B_4, B_10, B_25 and B_30 match the draft.  Tool caveats: - T1's 'powered envelope with an empty prefix is exactly the rational-eps CA bound' is loose. The repo theorem needs a prefix with last exponent A, so the empty prefix needs an explicitly certified cap. - T2 correctly quarantines SA-only caps from the pointwise envelope. - T5's closed form is correct and checkable, but it is the draft's own derivation and needs review.  The novelty assessment is fair. The genuinely new items are presentational or rigor-level: a self-contained directed certificate, the explicit 5583 threshold, the explicit boundary case, and the capped rational-eps subtree formulation. The open cut 'unbounded canonical tail' is equivalent to RH.  Robin 1984, Alaoglu-Erdos 1944, Briggs 2006 and Nicolas 1983 originals remain unread. The Briggs URL now serves spam, and the AMS PDF is blocked by a bot check. Their attributed statements rest on consistent secondary sources. No repository file was modified. My scratch scripts are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/verify_robin/.

**Missed prior art flagged:**
- K. Broughan, Equivalents of the Riemann Hypothesis, Vol. 1: Arithmetic Equivalents, Encyclopedia Math. Appl. 164, CUP 2017 (zbMATH 1427.11001): a book-length treatment of the Robin, Nicolas, Lagarias and SA/CA criteria. It is the natural single reference for the packet's 'Imported global source interface' and its classical context. (Existence and metadata checked on zbMATH; contents not read.)
- W. D. Banks, D. N. Hart, P. Moree, C. W. Nevans, 'The Nicolas and Robin inequalities with sums of two squares', Monatsh. Math. 157(4) (2009) 303-322 (zbMATH 1275.11130). This is another infinite family on which Robin holds and belongs in the PA10 baseline of parameter-restricted results. (Metadata checked only.)
- J.-L. Nicolas, 'The sum of divisors function and the Riemann hypothesis', Ramanujan J. 58(4) (2022) 1113-1157 (zbMATH 1497.11243). It gives effective forms of Ramanujan's RH-conditional bound (cited by Axler for this purpose) and is the modern reference for O2.
- Unconditional upper bounds bounding how near-extremal any violator must be: Robin 1984 Theoreme 2 (sigma(n)/n < e^g loglog n + 0.6483/loglog n), Axler 2023 Theorem 1.3 (factor 1+0.0094243/(loglog n)^3 for n not in an explicit finite set), and Hertlein 2018 Theorem 3. All are read via Axler/AF. They are not used in the draft's tools and are useful for finite-remainder accounting in T3/T4.
- OEIS A025487 (least integer of each prime signature, i.e. products of primorials, i.e. the 'Hardy-Ramanujan integers'), A004394 (SA) and A004490 (CA): the standard identification behind PA1 and PA7.
- A. Assani, A. Chester, A. Paschal, arXiv:2503.03159 (2025, unrefereed): elementary proofs that Robin holds for all numbers not divisible by one of 2, 3, 5, for primorials and for 21-free integers. It also shows the Kaneko-Lagarias inequality holds for all n iff it holds for all SA numbers.
- C. Mishra, R. Sarkar, arXiv:2609.26787 (Sept 2026, unrefereed): a truncated Robin inequality sigma(n)/n < e^g sum_{j<=omega(n)} (lnlnln n)^j/j!, proved equivalent to RH, proved unconditionally for omega(n)<=6, odd and squarefree n; its minimal counterexample is SA. This is recent work in the same 'arithmetic-tail' space.
- B. Zimov, arXiv:2510.23889 (2025) and arXiv:2611.13606 (Nov 2026), unrefereed: structure of the least CA counterexample (the quotient by its CA predecessor is a prime or semiprime) and constraints it places on zeta zeros. Relevant to T1 and to the PA4 CA route.
- P. Sole, Y. Zhu, 'An asymptotic Robin inequality', arXiv:1602.03384: liminf_{n} (e^g loglog n - sigma(n)/n) = 0 unconditionally. This is the unconditional counterpart of O2's shrinking-margin point. (Abstract only.)
- D. Platt, T. Trudgian, 'The Riemann hypothesis is true up to 3*10^12', Bull. LMS 53 (2021). The draft names it as an imported input for Axler and Morrill-Platt but gives no formal citation entry.


## SHARP_NATIVE

**Programme scope examined:** SHARP_NATIVE: signed native/Möbius sources. Covers SHARP powers (research/integrated/CURRENT_RESULTS.md §2), fixed-P61 bias (§8), the fixed Mellin consumer they feed (§3), and the packets research/integrated/{sharp_native, native_assets, native_endpoint, dickman_bellman}. Frozen proof bodies were read at their exact source SHAs via the GitHub API and copied to /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62a

### Known obstructions

- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-O1** Ingham's theorem (1942) applies to critical Möbius-type sums with a constant bias. If M(x)/sqrt x or L(x)/sqrt x is bounded on one side, then RH holds, the zeros are simple, and the zero ordinates satisfy infinitely many Z-linear relations. If there are no, or only finitely many, such relations, liminf = -infinity and limsup = +infinity. Mossinghoff-Trudgian and Humphries adapt the argument to L_1 (alpha=1, Turán's function). — *A. E. Ingham, Amer. J. Math. 64 (1942) 313-319 (zbMATH 0063.02974 review); the adaptation to alpha in [0,1/2) and alpha=1 is summarised in P. Humphries, arXiv:1108.1524, §1, and M. J. Mossinghoff and T. S. Trudgian, arXiv:2007.14537, §1.*. How it constrains: H_1 has constant apparent bias b_H ≈ 1.8033. Its zero coefficients have size about 1/(gamma\|zeta'(rho)\|), and their absolute sum appears to diverge like the Mertens sum (1.674 after 400 zeros). Eventual nonnegativity of H_1 would imply RH (L-99943) and, by the Ingham argument, Q-linear dependence of the ordinates; it is therefore expected false under LI. The adaptation to H_1 has not been checked line by line. The repo's negative-mass formulation of OPEN.ARITH.CV avoids this obstruction. Any route needing pointwise H_1>=0 (or a fixed-p deep-root sign with constant bias) should be treated as LI-obstructed. [abstract_only (Ingham review) + fulltext_read (summaries in Humphries and Mossinghoff-Trudgian) + analyst computation (h1coef.py); verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-O2** Finite positivity is unreliable at critical scale. Turán's T(x)=sum lambda(n)/n has positive apparent bias (sqrt x T(x) has bias -1/zeta(1/2)=0.685), yet it is first negative at x=72,185,376,951,205. Pólya's L(x) first changes sign at 906,150,257. — *C. B. Haselgrove, Mathematika 5 (1958) 141-145 (zbMATH 0085.27102); Borwein-Ferguson-Mossinghoff, Math. Comp. 77 (2008), as quoted in Angelo-Xu, arXiv:2205.05822, eq. (1.1); Tanaka, as quoted in Humphries §1.*. How it constrains: H_1>=1 on [1,1e7] (analyst exploratory computation) is not evidence for the critical estimate. H_1's bias is 2.6 times Turán's, so any negative values are expected only at astronomically large x. [fulltext_read (Angelo-Xu introduction) + abstract_only (Haselgrove review); verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-O3** Parity problem. Sieve axioms based on level of distribution (Type I, axiom R) cannot determine signed Möbius/prime sums, even at level x^{1-eps} (Selberg; Bombieri's asymptotic sieve). A bilinear hypothesis exploiting sign changes of mu(mn) is required. — *J. Friedlander and H. Iwaniec, 'Asymptotic sieve for primes', Ann. of Math. 148 (1998) 1041-1065, arXiv:math/9811186, §1 (pp.1041, 1044-1045); E. Bombieri, 'The asymptotic sieve', Mem. Accad. Naz. XL 1/2 (1976) 243-269.*. How it constrains: The dynamic critical Bellman block (fixed/small least prime) cannot be closed by propagating positivity through Buchstab/Bellman recursions fed only with rough-number densities, Dickman/Buchstab profiles or sieve bounds. A source-specific bilinear (Type II) Möbius input is unavoidable. Even such an input yields only log-power savings, not the x^{-1/2+eps} scale this RH-bearing root needs. [fulltext_read (FI introduction); Bombieri secondary_source; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-O4** RH-equivalence of the smooth-number asymptotic at y ≈ (log x)^{2+eps} (Hildebrand 1984). — *A. Hildebrand, Mathematika 31 (1984) 258-271 (zbMATH 0544.10042).*. How it constrains: Uniform growing-P control with relative margins once P extends to primes up to about (log X)^2 and beyond lives where the smooth-number analogue is RH-equivalent. At z near x, the P61-type object becomes the full critical detector. A bounded-envelope or Rankin-type argument cannot cross this threshold. [abstract_only; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-O5** Pretentious/Halász ceiling. For general multiplicative f with \|f\|<=1, one-sided bounds on sum f(n)/n are only of size (log log x)^{-3/5} to 1/log x, and negative values of size -c/log x are attained by some f in F1. — *Granville and Soundararajan, arXiv:math/0508361, Theorems 1-2.*. How it constrains: Any argument that uses only multiplicativity, \|f\|<=1 and prime-sum data cannot reach the power saving required by OPEN.ARITH.CV or FCHD67. Power savings need zero-free-strip information, i.e. RH-strength. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-O6** Limit of the subunit-mass method. The labelled-level contraction needs sum_p p^{-(m+1)/2}+67^{-(m+1)/2}<1, i.e. m>m*≈1.80207. At m=1 the relevant mass is the divergent prime-harmonic series (Mertens). — *Analyst computation (mpmath primezeta, h1bias.py); L-99613 §5 and R-99611 as quoted there.*. How it constrains: No variant of the Euler-cube/level-mass proof can reach m=1 without genuine cancellation input. The gap between m*≈1.80 and m=1 is structural, consistent with the repo's 'critical wall'. [analyst computation; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-O7** Unsmoothed partial sums sum_{n<=X} mu(n) and sum_{n<=X} mu(n)/n change sign infinitely often (Kaczorowski-Pintz). Log-smoothed sums at sigma=1 are nonnegative (Pólya/Turán/RZA). — *J. Kaczorowski and J. Pintz, 'Oscillatory properties of arithmetical functions I', Acta Math. Hungar. (1986), Cor. 4, as cited in arXiv:2312.05138 p.3.*. How it constrains: Sign targets must keep enough smoothing. Removing the ramp or kernel smoothing from a detector can turn a plausible sign statement into a false one. [secondary_source; verifier: confirmed]

### Transplantable tools

- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-T1** Legendre/Tao identity plus standard sieve lower bounds, giving an explicit and jointly uniform margin for prime-interval prefixes. (*Tao, arXiv:0908.4323, proof of Thm 1.1 and Thm 1.1 (\|A_P\|<=1); Phi(x,y)<<x/log y and the Fundamental Lemma (Halberstam-Richert, Sieve Methods, Thm 2.5; Friedlander-Iwaniec, Opera de Cribro §6); uniform Dickman asymptotics for bounded u (Hildebrand-Tenenbaum 1993 survey).*). Target gap: Required fix of ARITH.DICKMAN.FIXED_EXPONENT_INTERVALS (L-101220/L-101221, CONDITIONAL_EXACT): joint uniformity in endpoint ratio, moving u, continuity points, tails and positive margin.. Next step: Step 1: for x>=p, write A_{p,q}(x) >= N_P(x)/x - C/log p - 1/p. Step 2: bound N_P(x)/x below. For x<=q^K use N_P(x)>=Psi(x,p)>=x rho(KA)/2 for p>=p_1(K,A). For x>=q^K use the Fundamental Lemma with level sqrt x, giving N_P(x)>=x prod_{p<l<q}(1-1/l)(1-O(e^{-K/2})) >= x/(2A). Step 3: conclude A_{p,q}(x) >= min(rho(KA)/2, 1/(2A)) - C/log p for all real x>=1. Step 4: by Tao's bound, the L-101221 compensation terms are at most p^{-1/2}+q^{-1/2}+(pq)^{-1/2} <= 3p^{-1/2}, so B_{p,q}(x)>0 for p>=p_0(A) with an explicit margin, and the subsequence argument is no longer needed.. Risk: Low to moderate. The uniformity constants in the Fundamental Lemma for sieving an interval of primes, and the Psi lower bound for bounded u, must be pinned to exact cited statements. Making p_0(A) effective requires explicit sieve constants. This tool does not touch the fixed/small-prime deep root.
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-T2** All-X, all-q non-negativity of log-smoothed Möbius sums: 0 <= sum_{n<=X,(n,q)=1} mu(n)/n log(X/n) <= 1.00303 q/phi(q). Also Tao's uniform bound \|sum_{n<=x,(n,P)=1} mu(n)/n\| <= 1. (*Ramaré and Zuniga Alterman, arXiv:2312.05138, Theorem 1.2 and Lemma 4.1; Tao, arXiv:0908.4323, eq. (9).*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS §2: uniform growing-prime control of rough/smooth Möbius prefixes in the native rough state (L-98040/T-98050).. Next step: Integrate the L-98040 Stieltjes transfer by parts once more in log coordinates. With l_z(t)=sum_{m<=e^t,(m,P_{<z})=1} mu(m)/m log(e^t/m), which lies in [0, 1.00303 prod_{p<z}(1-1/p)^{-1}], this gives F(Y,z)/sqrt Y = l_z(log Y)h'(0+) + int l_z(log Y - v) dh'(v). Then record exactly which sign of the second log-derivative measure of the base would give positivity, and in which (Y,z) ranges.. Risk: High for the RH-bearing part. The critical information sits in l_z - K_z, which is signed and of size Y^{-1/2+eps} under RH. dh' is not one-signed when h is bounded, so positivity cannot follow from l_z>=0 alone. The tool mainly supplies uniform-in-z bounds and a cleaner bookkeeping of the base.
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-T3** Explicit-formula bias classification of fixed detectors (apparent/persistent bias; Ingham versus log-biased regimes). (*Martin-Mossinghoff-Trudgian, arXiv:2112.05227 §1 and Thm 3; Humphries, arXiv:1108.1524, Thms 1.5-1.6; Mossinghoff-Trudgian 2012.*). Target gap: Sign branches of OPEN.ARITH.ROWS23_NATIVE, FCHD67 and the Bellman deep root, as distinct from their negative-mass branches (OPEN_CUTS §1).. Next step: For each fixed detector (H_1; rows 2,3; the 5:3 scalar; the deep root U(Y,p) at fixed p), compute the residue at s=0 of N(s)/zeta(s+1/2) and the size of the zero coefficients. For H_1 the analyst gets b_H=1.8033 with coefficients ~1/(gamma zeta'(rho)), which is the Ingham regime. For a log-ramp row with a double pole the bias grows like log x, the Humphries alpha=1/2 regime, where the sign is plausibly RH-equivalent rather than LI-obstructed. For the deep root, if b(x)=a_*sqrt x+c_*+O(x^{-3/2}) as in L-98040.6, the RH-conditional constant term of F(Y,p) is c_*/(zeta(1/2) prod_{l<p}(1-l^{-1/2})), whose sign is -sign(c_*). Record which sign targets are expected false and which are plausibly RH-equivalent.. Risk: These are conditional (RH, simple zeros, LI or Gonek-Hejhal) heuristics and classifications, not estimates. The deep-root bias formula depends on the exact base expansion and endpoint conventions, which the analyst could not check locally (c_* is not numerically given in the resident files).
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-T4** Haselgrove / Odlyzko-te Riele / Hurst lattice-reduction disproof template for one-sided bounds, using Ingham's Fejér-kernel inequality with finitely many zeros. (*Haselgrove 1958 (zbMATH 0085.27102); G. Hurst, 'Computations of the Mertens function and improved bounds on the Mertens conjecture', Math. Comp. 87 (2018), arXiv:1610.08551 (abstract: liminf M/sqrt x < -1.837625, limsup > 1.826054).*). Target gap: Firewall for sign-type versions of the critical SHARP detector, so that no future route relies on 'H_1 eventually >= 0'.. Next step: Compute c_rho=(1-67^{-rho})(rho+1)/(zeta'(rho)(rho-1)(rho-1/2)) for the first few thousand zeros. Use LLL/Kronecker search to find y with b_H + sum_{\|gamma\|<T} k(gamma/T)·2Re(c_rho y^{i gamma}) < 0. If found, then by Ingham's inequality and L-99943, 'H_1 eventually >= 0' is unconditionally false.. Risk: Borderline feasibility. b_H=1.803 is just below Hurst's Mertens record 1.837, and the H_1 coefficient sums track the Mertens ones (1.674 versus 1.637 after 400 zeros). A computation may fail to produce a witness. The outcome is a firewall only and does not affect the negative-mass target.
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-T5** PNT-strength bounds for Möbius sums over smooth numbers (Alladi; Hildebrand's transfer inequality; VK), together with Rankin's trick. (*Hildebrand, Monatsh. Math. 97 (1984) 119-124; Alladi 1982; Rankin's trick (e.g. Tenenbaum §III.5); Hildebrand-Tenenbaum 1993 survey.*). Target gap: §8 open item 'uniform growing-P control' for F_z(x)=sum_{d\|P(z)} mu(d)A(x/d)/sqrt d.. Next step: Decompose F_z = 6 sum_{(N,P(z))=1} H_x(N)/sqrt N - 6S(x) + (9/sqrt2)S(x/2) - (3/2)S(x/4), where S(y)=sum_{d\|P(z)} mu(d)d^{-1/2}H_y(d). The first term is a positive rough count, about 12 sqrt x prod_{p<z}(1-1/p) by the Fundamental Lemma. Bound S(y) by partial summation from sum_{d<=t,P^+(d)<z} mu(d): Rankin/trivial bounds for u >= log log x, Hildebrand's inequality for intermediate u, and PNT/VK savings (<< t/log^A t) for bounded u and z <= x/K. This should give positivity of F_z(x) for x >= x_0(K) uniformly in z <= x/K. State explicitly that z >~ x/K turns F_z into the full critical detector. For that detector the analyst computes, under RH, a constant bias log4·(6-1.136/zeta(1/2)) ≈ 9.40 with zero coefficients O(1/(gamma^2\|zeta'(rho)\|)); this regime remains RH-bearing.. Risk: Moderate. A uniform smooth-Möbius saving across all u must be pinned to an exact published theorem (Alladi, Hildebrand 1987, or de la Bretèche-Tenenbaum 2005, none verified here). Constants are ineffective unless explicit VK is used. The top regime z ≈ x stays open.
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-T6** Rubinstein-Sarnak/Ng limiting-distribution machinery. (*N. Ng, arXiv:math/0310381; Humphries, arXiv:1108.1524, §§3-5.*). Target gap: OPEN.ARITH.CV: quantify, conditionally, what the true size of the critical negative mass is.. Next step: Under RH and J_{-1}(T)<<T, adapt Ng's weak-Mertens argument to show int_1^X (H_1-b_H)^2 dx/x << log X, hence N(X) << log X. Under LI in addition, compute the logarithmic density of {H_1<0}, which should be tiny and positive. Record this as the conditional benchmark the subpower target must match.. Risk: Purely conditional. It produces no unconditional progress and must never be presented as evidence for the estimate.
- **BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-T7** Friedlander-Iwaniec bilinear-form (Type II) hypothesis and asymptotic-sieve combinatorics. (*Friedlander and Iwaniec, Ann. of Math. 148 (1998), arXiv:math/9811186, §1, hypotheses (R) and (B).*). Target gap: Dynamic critical Bellman block (OPEN_CUTS §2): the parity-breaking input.. Next step: State the native analogue of hypothesis (B), cancellation of sum_m sum_n alpha_m beta_n mu(mn) over a range N in (Delta^{-1}D, delta^{-1}sqrt x), for the literal rough source. Determine which Bellman descendants it would control.. Risk: High. FI-type inputs give asymptotics with log-power savings, whereas the fixed-least-prime root requires RH-scale control. The tool clarifies what is missing but cannot by itself close the block.

### Novelty assessment

Classical or known in substance, though stated here for new specific objects: (a) The exact identities: the distributional descent L-99940/42 (the Riesz-means mechanism), the Buchstab/first-owner decomposition L-99601, and the Stieltjes transfer L-98040 (integration by parts). (b) The RH equivalence L-99944 and the subpower-negative-mass consumer (§3). These are the Littlewood criterion plus Landau's lemma (MV Lemma 15.1), as in Pólya/Turán/Mossinghoff-Trudgian/Alkan. (c) The fixed-P61 bias (§8). It is a fixed finite-modulus Legendre sieve of a near-constant ramp sequence; R25 itself disclaims novelty. The analyst checked that its asymptotic ratio prod(p-1)/(p+1)=1/35.2 lies inside [1/42,1/8]. (d) The mesoscopic Dickman/Bellman corridor. It is a signed analogue of Hildebrand's VK-range smooth-number asymptotic, with the narrower range forced by absolute errors. (e) The truncated Dickman profile F_a of L-101220. Via the Legendre/Tao identity it is the limiting density of integers free of primes in (p,p^a), so its positivity is immediate.  Apparently new as a stated theorem: L-99613 (all-x positivity of H^{[m]} for every real m>=2, with the 67-doubled Möbius source and the kernel T^m). No identical statement was found. The technique is the elementary Bonferroni/Brun inclusion-exclusion with subunit prime mass, in the absolutely convergent regime sigma=(m+1)/2>=3/2. Eventual positivity for each fixed m>1 is soft (dominated convergence). The literature already has a stronger-in-sigma all-X positivity of log-smoothed Möbius sums at sigma=1 (Pólya 1919, Turán 1948, Ramaré-Zuniga Alterman Thm 1.2), so L-99613 is a sound but modest result whose distance to RH is structural. The same proof seems to extend to m>m*≈1.802 (unreviewed observation).  Genuinely RH-strength and open: OPEN.ARITH.CV (RH-equivalent by the repo's own proof), FCHD67, ROWS23_NATIVE, the deep fixed-least-prime Bellman root, and top-range growing-P control. The literature supplies no tool that reaches these.  The literature does supply firewalls: - Ingham (1942) and the Martin-Mossinghoff-Trudgian apparent-bias analysis imply that pointwise eventual positivity of the critical detector H_1 is expected false under LI. H_1 has constant apparent bias b_H=1.8033, and its zero-coefficient sums track the Mertens ones (1.674 versus 1.637 after 400 zeros). So only negative-mass targets are viable for H_1. - The parity problem (Selberg; Bombieri; Friedlander-Iwaniec) rules out closing the dynamic Bellman block with sieve and density inputs alone.  Uncertainties: - Several classical attributions (Buchstab, Hardy-Riesz, Brun, Fundamental Lemma) are from memory. - The Hildebrand and Alladi statements are abstract-level. - The GS Thm 2 uniformity-in-P reading for squarefree-supported f is an inference that conflicts with Tao's Remark 1.2 and needs checking. - The deep-root bias sign depends on the constant c_*, which is not resident locally. - native_endpoint (F_Lambda) and native_assets were not compared, because their definitions are not available in resident files.

### Recommended actions

- Cite Montgomery-Vaughan Lemma 15.1 (Landau) and Littlewood (Titchmarsh §14.25) as the classical template behind L-99943/L-99944 and CURRENT_RESULTS §3. Add Mossinghoff-Trudgian (2012) and Alkan (2021) as the closest published analogues of an RH-equivalent weighted sign/mass criterion.
- Add an explicit firewall line to sharp_native/POWER_M_GE_2.md, as a proposed object for review rather than an edit of reviewed text. Under RH, H_1 = b_H + sum_rho c_rho x^{i gamma} + o(1) with apparent bias b_H=-3(1-67^{-1/2})/zeta(1/2)≈1.8033. By Ingham (1942), 'H_1 eventually >= 0' would force Q-linear relations among the zero ordinates, so OPEN.ARITH.CV must stay a negative-mass statement. Record that H_1>=1 on [1,1e7] (exploratory) is no evidence, compared with Turán's sum, whose bias is 0.685 and which is first negative at about 7.2e13.
- Classify each fixed detector by its RH explicit formula (T3) before pursuing a sign branch: constant bias with non-summable coefficients (H_1: LI-obstructed sign), log-growing bias (log-ramp rows: Humphries alpha=1/2-type, sign plausibly RH-equivalent), or constant bias with summable coefficients. Question for maintainers: does the 'eventual nonnegativity' option in OPEN.ARITH.ROWS23_NATIVE and the FCHD67 -> 'rows 2,3 nonnegative' arrow refer to a log-biased detector? If the bias were constant, the sign option would be LI-obstructed.
- Question for maintainers: compute the RH-conditional constant term of the deep Bellman root F(Y,p) at fixed p. With b(x)=a_*sqrt x+c_*+O(x^{-3/2}) (L-98040.6), it is c_*/(zeta(1/2) prod_{l<p}(1-l^{-1/2})), of sign -sign(c_*). If c_*>0, pointwise positivity of the fixed-p root is expected to fail on most large Y under RH. In either case Ingham/LI predicts sign changes, so the root target should be phrased as negative mass.
- Repair ARITH.DICKMAN.FIXED_EXPONENT_INTERVALS (CONDITIONAL_EXACT) with tool T1: the Legendre/Tao identity A_P(x)=N_P(x)/x+x^{-1}sum mu(d){x/d}, Phi(x,p)<<x/log p, the Fundamental Lemma, and the Psi(x,p) lower bound for bounded u. This yields A_{p,q}(x) >= min(rho(KA)/2, 1/(2A)) - C/log p for all real x, and Tao's \|A_P\|<=1 bounds the B_{p,q} compensation by 3p^{-1/2}. Submit it as a new proposed object with its own review, and note that F_a(u) is the limiting density of integers free of primes in (p,p^a).
- Cite Tao (2010, Bull. Aust. Math. Soc. 81) Theorem 1.1 wherever rough or interval reciprocal Möbius prefixes are bounded, in place of total-variation bounds prod(1+1/l). Cite Ramaré-Zuniga Alterman (arXiv:2312.05138) Theorem 1.2 / Lemma 4.1 for the uniform-in-q bounds 0 <= sum_{(n,q)=1} mu(n)/n log(X/n) <= 1.00303 q/phi(q), as tools for growing-P bookkeeping and explicit finite certificates.
- In CURRENT_RESULTS §2 or the SHARP manifest, add a scoped remark (as a proposed, separately reviewed object). First, for each fixed m>1, eventual positivity of H^{[m]} is immediate from x^{-m/2}H^{[m]}(x) -> 4^m(1-67^{-(m+1)/2})/zeta((m+1)/2)>0. Second, the labelled-level proof appears to cover every m>m*≈1.80207, where sum_p p^{-(m+1)/2}+67^{-(m+1)/2}=1. Third, cite the Pólya/Turán/Ramaré-Zuniga Alterman all-X non-negativity at sigma=1 as related, stronger-in-sigma prior art.
- For the §8 open item 'uniform growing-P control', split the problem by z/x (tool T5). The range z <= x/K is plausibly tractable at PNT strength (positive rough count plus smooth-Möbius savings by Hildebrand, Alladi or VK). For z >~ x/K the object is the full critical detector; the analyst computes an RH-conditional bias of about 9.40 for it. Register that top regime as RH-bearing, citing Hildebrand (1984, Mathematika 31) for the (log x)^2 RH threshold, which coincides with the A-audit kappa=1/2 boundary.
- Register the parity obstruction (Selberg; Bombieri 1976; Friedlander-Iwaniec 1998 §1) against the dynamic critical Bellman block. Any closure must import a bilinear (Type II) Möbius cancellation for the literal rough source, and even that gives only log-power savings at fixed least prime.
- Lock T-98050's classical inputs, as its required fix demands: (i) the de Bruijn asymptotic and log-derivative bounds for rho, via Hildebrand-Tenenbaum 1993 survey Thm 2.1 and Lemma 2.2; (ii) the VK prime-number-theorem error term with stated constants; (iii) Hildebrand 1986 as the benchmark range for counts. Clearly separate absolute VK errors from the relative errors used in the counting literature.
- Optional exploratory computation (T4): run a Haselgrove / Odlyzko-te Riele / Hurst-style lattice search with Ingham's kernel on c_rho=(1-67^{-rho})(rho+1)/(zeta'(rho)(rho-1)(rho-1/2)) to test whether 'H_1 eventually >= 0' is unconditionally false. Label the outcome as a firewall only, never as progress on the negative-mass target.
- Before citing Granville-Soundararajan Theorem 2 for a uniform lower bound -(log log x)^{-3/5} on sum_{n in <P>, n<=x} mu(n)/n, verify the 2-adic exceptional condition for squarefree-supported f and the uniformity of the implied constants in the published version. Tao's Remark 1.2 quotes -0.4553 for this class instead.

### Independent verifier

Overall the draft is careful and well scoped. Every literature source cited for a prior-art or obstruction item exists with the stated bibliographic data. These were checked in full text (Tao, RZA, GS, MMT, Humphries, FI, Angelo-Xu, MT 2020) or through zbMATH reviews (Ingham, Haselgrove, Hildebrand ×3, MT 2012, Alkan) and the HT survey. All load-bearing numerics were reproduced: S_2, m*, the pure-mu threshold 1.399433, b_H, the P61 ratio 1/35.21, the 400-zero coefficient sums, and the H_1 minimum and log-average. The repository statements match the frozen sources in sharp_src and CLAIMS.tsv.  Several corrections are needed. (1) PA1 and the novelty assessment call RZA/Pólya/Turán 'stronger-in-sigma' prior art for L-99613. That framing is misleading. Log-Riesz positivity at sigma=1 depends on the kernel vanishing at the endpoint. T^m does not vanish there (H^{[m]} jumps at every integer), and L-99613 is an absolute-convergence, subunit-prime-mass phenomenon. They are the same genre, but neither implies the other. RZA also refer to Pólya and Turán only 'for historical discussion'. (2) The PA9 'discrepancy' with Tao's Remark 1.2 is not real. For squarefree-supported mu·1_<P>, the 2-adic exceptional set of GS Theorem 2 is empty, so the uniform -(loglog x)^{-3/5} bound applies. (3) PA15 and novelty item (c) misread R25. Its 'not a claim of mathematical novelty' means the repair adds no theorem beyond L-97400; it does not disclaim literature novelty. (4) PA14 is over-related. Alkan's pole-dominated regime corresponds in SHARP coordinates to 0<m<1, where, under RH, H^{[m]} has a negative x^{m/2} main term. This yields a natural (unreviewed) RH-equivalent sign criterion that the draft missed. It is not the L-99940 critical descent. (5) O3 understates the unconditional savings: VK beats every log power. The parity barrier should also be labelled heuristic; the binding obstruction at fixed least prime is RH-scale. (6) O4 and PA11 can be upgraded in evidence but downgraded in relation. Hildebrand's (log x)^2 RH threshold and the F14 kappa=1/2 boundary share a square-root-scale origin, and F14 itself proves no failure there.  Some transplantable tools were spot-checked. In T1, Tao's identity gives A_{p,q}=N_P/x+O(1/log p) for x>=p, and the compensation terms are bounded by 3p^{-1/2}; the plan is sound, pending exact cited forms of the Fundamental Lemma and of the Psi lower bound. For T5, the full-detector bias was re-derived from the Mellin transform Q(s+1/2)(1-4^{-s})/(s^2 zeta(s+1/2)): it is log4·(6+R(0)/\|zeta(1/2)\|) with R(0)=-1.136, about 9.40. The zero coefficients are O(1/(gamma^2\|zeta'(rho)\|)), so that regime has summable coefficients and is not Ingham-obstructed. The deep-root bias formula in T3 is consistent with the Mellin poles of b. Using Tanaka/MMT Theorem 3 and Mossinghoff-Trudgian 2017 as the unconditional oscillation template, rather than only the Haselgrove/Hurst lattice route, should be added to T4.

**Missed prior art flagged:**
- M. J. Mossinghoff and T. S. Trudgian, 'The Liouville function and the Riemann hypothesis', in Exploring the Riemann Zeta Function (Springer, 2017) 201-221, doi:10.1007/978-3-319-59969-4_9. This gives unconditional Ingham-kernel/lattice oscillation bounds for weighted Liouville sums, e.g. L_1(x)sqrt(x)>2.37 infinitely often (quoted in arXiv:2007.14537 §1). It is the direct template for T4 and for quantitative unconditional statements about H_1-b_H.
- M. J. Mossinghoff and T. S. Trudgian, 'Oscillations in weighted arithmetic sums', arXiv:2007.14537. It proves unconditional large oscillations of H_alpha(x)x^{alpha-1/2} for every alpha in [0,1]. This is the closest published model for unconditional Omega± results on a constant-bias weighted multiplicative sum. The draft uses only its §1 summary.
- M. Tanaka, 'On the Möbius and allied functions', Tokyo J. Math. 3 (1980) 215-218 (Zbl 0451.10029), and its generalisation in MMT Fake mu's §4. The first-zero Landau argument applied to Mellin(H_1)(s)=(s+3/2)(1-67^{-s-1/2})/(s(s-1/2)zeta(s+1/2)) should give, unconditionally, that H_1-b_H changes sign infinitely often. This is my derivation and is unreviewed.
- A. M. Odlyzko and H. J. J. te Riele, 'Disproof of the Mertens conjecture', J. reine angew. Math. 357 (1985) 138-160 (Zbl 0544.10047). This is the original lattice-reduction/Ingham-kernel method that T4 proposes, and which Hurst only refines.
- P. Borwein, R. Ferguson and M. J. Mossinghoff, 'Sign changes in sums of the Liouville function', Math. Comp. 77 (2008) 1681-1694 (Zbl 1198.11090). This is the primary source for the Turán first-negative value in O2, which the draft cites only via Angelo-Xu.
- E. Alkan, J. Number Theory 225 (2021) 90-124, its Möbius-function criteria (mentioned in the zbMATH review but omitted by the draft). In SHARP coordinates sigma=(m+1)/2 in (1/2,1), i.e. 0<m<1. Under RH, x^{-m/2}H^{[m]}(x) tends to 4^m(1-67^{-sigma})/zeta(sigma)<0, so the sign of SHARP powers flips across m=1. 'H^{[m]} eventually negative for all m in (0,1)' is then a plausible RH-equivalent. This needs a noncancellation check for K_m(s) and is my derivation, unreviewed.
- J. B. Friedlander, 'Integers free from large and small primes', Proc. London Math. Soc. (3) 33 (1976) 565-576 (Zbl 0344.10021), and the Buchstab-Dickman literature on integers with no prime factor in an interval. These are the classical setting in which F_a(u) of L-101220 (PA8) is a sieve density.
- A. Granville and K. Soundararajan, 'Decay of mean values of multiplicative functions', Canad. J. Math. 55 (2003) 1191-1230. It is the input [2, Cor. 3] behind GS Proposition 3.1, and it supplies the uniform slow-variation estimates needed if GS-type one-sided bounds are used for rough prefixes.
- R. Angelo and M. W. Xu, 'On a Turán conjecture and random multiplicative functions', arXiv:2205.05822, Theorem 1.1: for a random completely multiplicative f, all truncations of sum f(n)/n are positive with probability >=1-10^{-45}. This is a probabilistic analogue of all-x positivity and a caution against reading finite positivity as structural.
- R. de la Bretèche and G. Tenenbaum, 'Entiers friables: inégalité de Turán-Kubilius et applications', Invent. Math. 159 (2005). It is named only in the draft's T5 risk note. It should be pinned down (not verified here) as the uniform smooth-number Möbius input for growing-P control.


## XI_PICK

**Programme scope examined:** XI_PICK: xi geometry, finite Pick/Loewner witnesses, low-order safe-axis (real s-axis) Pick positivity, and xi derivative descent. Covers CURRENT_RESULTS.md section 6, OPEN_CUTS.md sections 3 and 4, research/integrated/xi/, xi_pick/, safe_line/ and finite_certificates/, plus the linked proof sources (reviews/C/pass4-math-completion/proofs/XI_SOURCE_REPAIR.md, reviews/A/supplement/REPORT.md S04-S06

### Known obstructions

- **BRG-XI_PICK-O1** Matrix-monotone classes have strict gaps P_{n+1}(I)⊊P_n(I) for every n and every interval, so positivity at a fixed finite order never implies the next order abstractly. — *F. Hansen, G. Ji, J. Tomiyama, Gaps between classes of matrix monotone functions, Bull. London Math. Soc. 36 (2004) 53-58, arXiv:math/0204204 (introduction read); W. F. Donoghue, Monotone Matrix Functions and Analytic Continuation, Springer 1974.*. How it constrains: Loewner-hierarchy counterpart of the repo's 'order three does not bootstrap abstractly' and of the X9 countermodel. Via Audenaert's order transfer (Kwong order 2N implies Loewner order N for tp and p), any order-4 proof must use source-specific information, as the order-3 proof did, not an abstract extension lemma. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-XI_PICK-O2** Information dispersal in finite-order hierarchies. Chasse: if all zeros with \|gamma\|<T are on the line, then J^{d,0} has only real zeros for d<T^2. Farmer's model X_10 (cos z with two zero pairs moved to ±10±i) has hyperbolic J^{d,0} for d<=118, and all zeros of its first derivative are real. — *D. W. Farmer, Jensen polynomials are not a plausible route to proving the Riemann Hypothesis, Adv. Math. 411 (2022) 108781, arXiv:2008.07206, sections 2, 4.1, 4.2 and Table 4.1; M. Chasse, Complex Var. Elliptic Equ. 58 (2013).*. How it constrains: The repo's X9 model already shows safe-axis order 3 cannot see an off-line quartet at height 1025. It is unknown whether the safe-axis Kwong hierarchy disperses information like Jensen polynomials (order ~T^2) or concentrates it like Sra's PF orders (PF_k forces RH up to height Y with N(Y)<=k+1). Under Farmer's criteria (A/B/C), OPEN_CUTS section 3 is a repackaging of RH whose usefulness depends on that rate. X_10 is also an explicit counterexample to naive derivative descent: f has nonreal zeros while f' is real-rooted. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-XI_PICK-O3** High-derivative behaviour is universal and RH-blind. Kim 1996 (fixed windows become real-rooted for any real entire function of order <2 with zeros in a strip), Ki 2006 (cosine limit), Gunns-Hughes 2019 (the same limit in the extended Selberg class, which contains RH-violating functions), Pemantle-Subramanian 2017 (random Poisson zeros) and Campbell-O'Rourke-Renfrew 2024 (cosine universality via finite free probability). — *Farmer arXiv:2008.07206 section 2 (summarizes Kim 1996, Ki 2006, Gunns-Hughes J. Number Theory 194 (2019) 30-43); R. Pemantle, S. Subramanian, Trans. AMS 369 (2017) 8743-8764, arXiv:1409.7956; A. Campbell, S. O'Rourke, D. Renfrew, arXiv:2410.06403.*. How it constrains: The repo's high-derivative entry and safe-ray theorems sit in a regime that holds whether or not RH is true. All RH content must therefore be carried by the descent step (OPEN_CUTS section 4). This is consistent with the repo's own scope statements and makes clear that the entry regime cannot help with the descent loss budget. [secondary_source; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-XI_PICK-O4** Monotonicity of the de Bruijn-Newman constant along derivatives, and Lambda>=0. The constants lambda^(m) for Xi_lambda^(m) are nonincreasing in m with limit <=0, and for every lambda>0 all but finitely many zeros of Xi_lambda are real and simple (Ki-Kim-Lee). Rodgers-Tao prove Lambda>=0. — *H. Ki, Y.-O. Kim, J. Lee, On the de Bruijn-Newman constant, Adv. Math. 222 (2009) 281-306 (zbMATH 1175.30023 review); B. Rodgers, T. Tao, The de Bruijn-Newman constant is non-negative, Forum Math. Pi 8 (2020), arXiv:1801.05914.*. How it constrains: Differentiation can only improve real-rootedness, so descent runs against the monotone direction. If RH holds, Lambda^(0)=0 exactly: there is no uniform positive margin at the base level. A descent or perturbation argument with a fixed strict margin at order 0 cannot exist; losses must be quantified down to zero margin. [secondary_source; verifier: confirmed_with_correction]
- **BRG-XI_PICK-O5** de Branges's positivity conditions for the space H(xi(1-iz)) are not satisfied; Conrey-Li show this for zeta and for the real character chi_-4. — *J. B. Conrey, X.-J. Li, A note on some positivity conditions related to zeta- and L-functions, IMRN 2000 no. 18, 929-940, arXiv:math/9812166 (abstract); discussed in Lagarias arXiv:math/0601653 section 6.*. How it constrains: Bears on the 'direct infinite canonical/Hermite-Biehler system for xi' that integration notes list as a live direction. Any such route must avoid de Branges's specific sufficient conditions, which are known to fail. [abstract_only; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-XI_PICK-O6** Positive definite real Hankel matrices are exponentially ill-conditioned: the spectral condition number grows at least geometrically in the order (Tyrtyshnikov; Beckermann). — *B. Beckermann, The condition number of real Vandermonde, Krylov and positive definite Hankel matrices, Numer. Math. 85 (2000) 553-577; E. E. Tyrtyshnikov, How bad are Hankel matrices?, Numer. Math. 67 (1994).*. How it constrains: The GAP_BLOCKED growing-order claim (eventual matrix monotonicity of the Xi impedance to growing order) cannot be closed by additive perturbation of Dobsch-Hankel matrices with order-independent error. The relative margin of the model shrinks geometrically, so a normalized moving frame (as in Sra Thm C.9) or a structural argument is required. [memory_unverified; verifier: confirmed]
- **BRG-XI_PICK-O7** Pole obstruction for PF orders: if Theta or Lambda is PF_k, every zero below roughly the (k+2)-nd ordinate is on the line, i.e. PF_k and N(Y)<=k+1 imply RH through height Y. Unconditional PF orders are therefore bounded by what zero verification has certified. — *S. Sra arXiv:2611.02944, Thm C.11 and Cor C.12.*. How it constrains: Benchmark for the Pick programme. Any unconditional finite-order safe-axis Kwong theorem is bounded by the verified height if the hierarchy concentrates information; if it disperses (OB2), finite orders cost nothing but say little. The repo should determine which regime applies before investing in order >=4. [fulltext_read; verifier: confirmed]

### Transplantable tools

- **BRG-XI_PICK-T1** Signed-perturbation scheme with an order-independent bound. Replace each unverified squared rate lambda_rho=(gamma-i delta_rho)^2 by gamma^2; this gives an exact PF_inf (here: Stieltjes) model times a signed factor M_U with weighted variation epsilon_U(Q)<1 (Lemma C.4). Then use a moving lower-triangular frame C_x with C_x H_m[f](x) C_x^T = J_m and \|\|C_x H_m[f](x-u) C_x^T\|\|<=e^{Qu} (Thm C.9), so all determinant signs up to order m reduce to scalar inequalities (Thm 3.4: n>=8m verified factors with rates <=Q). (*S. Sra arXiv:2611.02944, Lemma C.4, Thm C.9, Thm 3.4, eq. (3.2), Appendix C.10.*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS section 3 / OPEN.OPERATOR.XI.PICK_ORDER4_PLUS at each fixed order N>=4, and OPERATOR.XI.FRACTIONAL_STRING_GROWING_ORDER (GAP_BLOCKED: 'supply quantified moving-band Vandermonde and perturbation bounds uniform in order').. Next step: Step 1: write p=p_real+p_corr, with p_real(t)=Sum_{all rho} 2m/(t+gamma^2), which is exactly Stieltjes and hence Kwong-PSD in every order, and p_corr the sum over unverified orbits of 2Re[2m/(t+lambda_rho)-2m/(t+gamma^2)], each term O(m gamma/(t+gamma^2)^2). Step 2: take the Dobsch-Hankel matrices M_N(t;tp) (Heinavaara) or, directly, the Cauchy-Gram form of the Kwong kernel, Sum c_l Re[v_l v_l^*] with v_l=(1/(x_i+i sqrt(a_l))). Orthonormalize them in a frame built from n>=8N verified atoms with a_l<=Q. Step 3: prove an analogue of (C.19), i.e. a bound on the frame-normalized p_corr contribution that is uniform in t>0 and in N, compared against Sum_{gamma>U} gamma^-2<=2(log U+1)/U. Start with N=4 as a bounded contribution.. Risk: Pick/Kwong kernels are Cauchy-type, not translation-invariant Toeplitz, so Sra's e^{Qu} shift bound has no direct analogue; the natural variable is multiplicative. Audenaert's order transfer goes only from Kwong order 2N to Loewner order N, so proving N-monotonicity of tp and p alone does not give Kwong order N; a direct Kwong-kernel argument may be needed. Large-t behaviour (t>>H^2, where the verified atoms degenerate toward a=0 in rescaled coordinates) may break uniformity.
- **BRG-XI_PICK-T2** Audenaert's exact anti-Loewner/Loewner correspondence: K_g+L_g = 2 Loewner[sqrt(x) g(sqrt x)](x_i^2) and K_g-L_g = -2 D Loewner[g(sqrt x)/sqrt x] D, together with the sign-independence of det[(s_i g_i+s_j g_j)/(s_i x_i+s_j x_j)] (Prop 2, Thm 2.2, proved by partial Gaussian elimination X=DYD). (*K. M. R. Audenaert, Proc. AMS 139 (2011) 4217-4223, arXiv:1008.2943, Prop 2, Thm 2.1, Thm 2.2, eq. (3).*). Target gap: Order-4 factorization attempt (L-92000 generalization); pinning OPERATOR.XI.LOEWNER_LOW_ORDER ('reciprocal Loewner-Hankel congruence') to a citable identity; precise statement of the all-order criterion L-91904.. Next step: Apply Audenaert's elimination (eq. 3) to the 4x4 Kwong matrix of F with g_i=x_i p(x_i^2). Compute the Schur complement Y symbolically and test whether det K_4 factors into divided differences of 1/p and tp, as the 3x3 case does, or into 2x2 Loewner determinants of tp and p. Then record in the xi_pick packet: Kwong order 2N implies tp N-monotone and p N-monotone decreasing.. Risk: The 4x4 determinant may not factor into scalar curvatures. Audenaert notes that no sufficiency argument at fixed N is known.
- **BRG-XI_PICK-T3** Dobsch-Donoghue local characterization as proved by Heinavaara: f in C^{2n-1} is n-monotone on (a,b) iff M_n(t)=[f^(i+j-1)(t)/(i+j-1)!]_{i,j<=n} is PSD for every t (similarly for n-convexity with the Kraus/Hankel matrix). The proof uses integral representations linking Loewner matrices to Hankel matrices; n-monotonicity is a local property. (*O. Heinavaara, Local characterizations for the matrix monotonicity and convexity of fixed order, Proc. AMS 146 (2018), arXiv:1608.07516, Thms 5 and 7; Donoghue 1974 Chs. 7-8.*). Target gap: OPERATOR.XI.FRACTIONAL_STRING_FIXED_ORDER (state 'eventual n-monotonicity on [T_n,inf)' as pointwise Hankel positivity with explicit T_n) and the growing-order GAP_BLOCKED row.. Next step: Write tp(t)~c sqrt(t) log t plus zero corrections. Express M_n(t;tp) relative to the beta-Hankel matrix of t^{1/2}, which is positive definite with a known Hilbert-type structure, and give explicit T_n with M_n(t)>0 for t>=T_n. Because n-monotonicity is local, gluing [T_n,inf) with a separate certificate on (0,T_n] would give global n-monotonicity of tp. That is a necessary condition for Kwong order 2n.. Risk: Gives Loewner (not Kwong) positivity. Explicit T_n may grow very fast (OB6). It controls tp only; p (monotone decreasing) needs a parallel argument.
- **BRG-XI_PICK-T4** Height-to-order calibration: Chasse's theorem (verification to T gives Jensen hyperbolicity for d<T^2) and Sra's pole obstruction (Thm C.11/Cor C.12), combined with Krein-Langer negative-square counting. (*Farmer arXiv:2008.07206 section 4.2; Sra arXiv:2611.02944 Thm C.11 and Cor C.12; Krein-Langer 1977.*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS section 3: determine whether finite-order safe-axis Pick positivity carries RH information (OB2 versus OB7).. Next step: For the X9-type model (critical pair at gamma0=1, one off-line quartet (a,b,m)=(1/4,b,1)), compute with exact rational arithmetic the minimal packet size N(b) at which some real-node Kwong matrix has a negative eigenvalue, for b=2^k up to about 2^12. A logarithmic N(b) means the hierarchy concentrates information (worth pursuing); a polynomial N(b) means it disperses (deprioritize per Farmer).. Risk: This is a diagnostic, not a theorem. Ill-conditioning (OB6) requires certified linear algebra; the repo's LDL^* / moat pipeline can be reused.
- **BRG-XI_PICK-T5** Sra's all-order zero-exclusion principle (Lemma 4.2): for real entire f of genus <=1 and an interval I where \|x-Re zeta\|>=\|Im zeta\| for every nonreal zero, the generalized Laguerre coefficients L_n[f](x) (coefficients of y^{2n} in \|f(x+iy)\|^2) are >=0 for all n. Consequently RH verified to height T gives L_n[Xi]>=0 for all n on \|x\|<=T-1/2 (Thm 1.3). (*S. Sra arXiv:2611.02944, Lemma 4.2, Thm 1.3; G. Csordas, R. S. Varga, Adv. Appl. Math. 11 (1990) 328-357, Thm 2.9; Csordas-Ruttan-Varga 1991.*). Target gap: safe_line OPEN.OPERATOR.RADIAL_CURVATURE; the general-height RH-necessary tests (CURRENT_RESULTS section 6) as a locality/complement theorem; and derivative descent, where a Fourier critical point of f^(n) is exactly a violation of L_1[f^(n-1)]>=0 at a zero of f^(n).. Next step: (a) Check whether radial curvature (T-91002) is a reparametrization of Jensen's convexity criterion d^2/dy^2\|Xi(x+iy)\|^2>=0 (Polya 1927 condition I'). If so, cite Jensen/Polya and import Sra Thm 1.3 for the verified range. (b) Test whether the product-coefficient argument of Lemma 4.2 adapts to show: for \|T\|<=H-c, all-order Pick positivity at height T holds whenever the nodes keep a geometric separation from every unverified zero. This would be a genuine locality theorem for the finite Pick boxes.. Risk: Pick positivity, unlike Laguerre positivity, is not local (a negative square is visible from any open set, PA15). So (b) likely gives only an 'approximately local' statement with exponentially small, not zero, error.
- **BRG-XI_PICK-T6** Fourier-Polya critical-point theory: the number of nonreal zeros equals twice the total number of critical points over all derivatives (Ki-Kim 2000 for genus 0; Kim 1996 for zeros in a strip with convergence exponent of nonreal zeros <1-rho/2). (*H. Ki, Y.-O. Kim, Duke Math. J. 104 (2000) 45-73 (Thms 4.1-4.3, per zbMATH review); Y.-O. Kim, Proc. AMS 124 (1996) 819-830 and J. Math. Anal. Appl. 204 (1996) 472-481.*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS section 4 (derivative descent) and F01 (reverse-Rolle inertia).. Next step: Restate descent as: RH holds iff Xi^(n) has no Fourier critical points for every n>=0, valid in any class where the Fourier-Polya count is proved. Check whether Ki-Kim Thm 4.2/4.3 covers order-1, genus-1 functions with zeros in \|Im z\|<1/2, such as Xi or G(t)=xi(1/2+sqrt t) after the change of variable. If not, record exactly which hypothesis fails (e.g. Kim's convergence-exponent<1/2 condition). That gives the repo's 'nonreal-critical block' a classical frame and a sharp boundary.. Risk: The general Fourier-Polya theorem may not cover Xi without a sparsity hypothesis on off-line zeros. If it does, the reformulation is still RH-equivalent, not a proof.
- **BRG-XI_PICK-T7** Lagarias's Hermite-Biehler shift family E_h(z)=xi(1/2+h-iz) and de Branges's Lemma 5 (\|E(s)\|>\|E(1-conj s)\| on Re s>1/2 implies A and B have only real, interlacing zeros). (*J. C. Lagarias, arXiv:math/0601653, Lemmas 2.1, 2.2 and 6.1.*). Target gap: OPEN_CUTS section 4: an alternative entry regime to high derivatives, and a test of the remark that raw innerness implies RH.. Next step: Prove or cite the equivalence 'E_h is Hermite-Biehler iff xi has no zeros with Re s>1/2+h'. If it holds, shift descent h->0 is exactly the zero-free half-plane problem (cf. Freitas arXiv:math/0507368, a Li-type criterion for zero-free half-planes). Compare its loss budget with the frozen-lambda safe-ray E_{r,lambda} to decide which entry regime has the better descent algebra.. Risk: Likely shows only that shift descent is equivalent to classical zero-free-region improvements, a known hard problem. Its value is clarifying, not resolving.

### Novelty assessment

Classical or known (high confidence): (i) Every RH-necessary inequality in research/integrated/xi/derivative-free-pick-loewner.md follows from three standard facts once the resolvent representation is accepted. Those facts are: under RH, xi'/xi is a Caratheodory function on Re s>1/2 (Hinkkanen 1997; Lagarias 1999); its Poisson/real part makes J_T complete Bernstein and H_T Stieltjes; and Cauchy-kernel total positivity applies (Karlin). This covers the secant, alternating divided differences, cross-Loewner total nonnegativity and the barycentric localizer. (ii) The scalar base R_T>=0 is Sondow-Dumitrescu horizontal monotonicity. (iii) The all-order real-node safe-axis criterion (L-91904, OPEN.OPERATOR.XI.PICK_ORDER4_PLUS taken over all orders) is an instance of Audenaert's 2011 characterization of anti-Loewner functions plus the identity theorem. It is equivalent to Sra 2026 Prop A.1(vi) (log h complete Bernstein), to Polson 2026 (complete monotonicity of the heat trace) and to Zhang 2023. The repo's H_ij is literally the anti-Loewner/Kwong matrix of F. (iv) The reverse-Rolle identity is a one-step form of classical Fourier-Polya critical-point counting. (v) The fixed-rectangle high-derivative entry is a special case of Kim 1996. (vi) The Conrey trial sits inside Conrey 1983.  Probably new, with honest uncertainty: (a) The specific finite disproof architecture (two-channel localizer, exact matched-pole annihilator) and the two directed 8x8 complex Pick boxes at T about 4.7e12. I found no literature computing certified complex Pick matrices for xi'/xi; Grigutis et al. work only at the scalar level. (b) Order-3 safe-axis Kwong positivity for actual xi via reciprocal concavity of p and the 3x3 determinant factorization. I found no prior statement. However, the order-2 part and the tp-curvature part follow, by a short Laplace-transform argument (mine, not stated in the source), from H>0 and H'<0. Sra 2026 and Polson 2026 prove that unconditionally using zeros only up to height 100, after the repo's August 2026 integration. Reciprocal concavity (1/p concave) is not implied by H>0, H'<0, so that component and the order-3 assembly remain the repo's distinctive contribution as far as I can tell. The critical-reserve budget mechanism is the same as Sra's Lemma 2.2 and eq. (2.2), down to the identical bound 2(log U+1)/U. It was found independently and earlier by the repo. (c) The growing high-derivative window c sqrt(m/log m) with frozen-lambda safe-ray sector control is a natural saddle-point strengthening of Kim 1996 and Ki 2006 for Xi. It may already be implicit in O'Sullivan 2020 or Coffey 2009 asymptotics, which I did not read in full.  Context that affects how to read the frontier: neighbouring hierarchies of the same function G(t)=xi(1/2+sqrt t) are unconditionally known far beyond order 3. These are Jensen degree <=8 for all shifts (GORZ), Jensen degree up to 2*10^17 at the origin (Chasse), and PF_{1.8*10^12} for the reciprocal laws (Sra). By analogy, fixed-order N>=4 safe-axis Pick positivity is very likely provable from Platt-Trudgian with the same signed-perturbation ideas. It should not be presented as an RH-strength frontier; only the all-order statement is RH-equivalent. Whether the Pick hierarchy disperses or concentrates zero information (Farmer's criterion) is open and is the key question for its usefulness.  I did not find an explicit error in any repository statement. Points I would put to contributors as questions, not defects: whether T-91002 (radial curvature) is a reparametrization of Jensen's 1913 / Polya 1927 convexity criterion; and whether Hinkkanen's 1997 matrices A_n coincide with the repo's Pick matrices.

- Cite Hinkkanen 1997 ('On functions of bounded type', Complex Var. 34, 119-139) next to Lagarias 1999 in xi/derivative-free-pick-loewner.md and finite-pick-controls.md as the earliest Pick-interpolation RH criterion. Obtain the paper and record whether its matrices A_n equal the repo's K_ij (nodes, normalization, denominator s+conj(w)-1).
- Cite Sondow-Dumitrescu 2010 (arXiv:1005.1104, Cor 1) and Matiyasevich-Saidak-Zvengrowski 2014 (arXiv:1205.2773) for 'R_T(x)>0 for all x,T iff RH', noting R_T(x)=d/dx log\|xi(1/2+x+iT)\|. Cite Lagarias 1999's result inf_t Re(xi'/xi)(sigma+it)=(xi'/xi)(sigma) for sigma>10 as context for the safe-axis choice.
- In xi_pick/ORDER_THREE_CANONICAL.md and for L-91904, cite Audenaert 2011 (arXiv:1008.2943): H_ij is the anti-Loewner matrix of F. The all-order criterion is his eq. (2) with alpha=beta=0. Record his exact order transfer (Kwong order 2N implies tp N-monotone and p N-monotone decreasing via K±L identities) and check PR #708's 'reciprocal Loewner-Hankel congruence' against Audenaert Thm 2.2.
- Add Sra 2026 (arXiv:2611.02944) and Polson 2026 (arXiv:1804.10043v9; arXiv:1708.02653v24) as parallel later work in xi_pick/README.md, recording repo priority dates (integration 2026-08-22). Note in a separately labelled proposed object, needing its own review, that H>0 and H'<0 (Sra Thm 1.1) imply OPERATOR.XI.PICK_ORDER2 and the tp-curvature half of order three via p=2*Integral e^{-su}H(u)du. State that reciprocal concavity is the order-3 component not covered.
- Split the xi-Pick part of OPEN_CUTS section 3 into: (a) 'fixed order N=4 (then N=5, ...) safe-axis Kwong positivity from Platt-Trudgian', listed as a bounded contribution that is probably provable and not RH-equivalent, with Sra's Lemma C.4/Thm C.9 moving-frame perturbation as the suggested tool; (b) the all-order statement, which is RH-equivalent and known in equivalent forms (Sra Prop A.1, Polson Thm 26, Zhang 2023, Audenaert plus Lagarias/Hinkkanen).
- Before investing in order >=4, run the diagnostic in TT4: for an X9-type model, compute the minimal Kwong packet size N(b) that detects an off-line quartet at height b, and compare with Chasse's T^2 dispersal and Sra's PF concentration (Cor C.12). Use Farmer 2022 (arXiv:2008.07206) section 4.1 criteria to decide whether the hierarchy is a useful repackaging.
- For OPERATOR.XI.FRACTIONAL_STRING_FIXED_ORDER and GROWING_ORDER, cite the Dobsch-Donoghue-Heinavaara local Hankel characterization (arXiv:1608.07516) and Hansen-Ji-Tomiyama (arXiv:math/0204204) for strict gaps. Record Hankel ill-conditioning (Beckermann 2000) as why order-independent additive bounds fail, and Sra's moving-frame Thm C.9 as the model for the 'uniform in order' estimate the GAP_BLOCKED row asks for.
- In RESULTS.md #high-derivatives and reviews/A/FINAL_AUDIT.md F02, cite Kim 1996 (fixed-window real zeros of f^(n) for real entire order<2 with zeros in a strip), Ki 2006 (cosine limit), Conrey 1983 (proportion 1-O(n^-2)), Farmer-Rhoades 2005, and Gunns-Hughes 2019 / Farmer 2022. Describe the entry theorem as a growing-window strengthening of Kim for Xi, and add the RH-blindness of the entry regime (OB3) and Farmer's X_10 example (f' real-rooted, f not) to the descent boundary text.
- In F01, cite the classical Fourier-Polya critical-point counting (Polya 1930; Ki-Kim, Duke 104 (2000); Kim 1996) for N_nr(p)-N_nr(p')=2E(p). Check whether Ki-Kim Thms 4.2-4.3 or Kim's strip theorem cover Xi, and record exactly which hypothesis (e.g. convergence exponent of nonreal zeros <1/2) blocks a direct application.

If so, re-label it as a known equivalence and import Sra 2026 Thm 1.3 (all generalized Laguerre inequalities on \|x\|<=T-1/2 given verification to T) for the verified range.
